115年 10月賦稅收入初步統計 
一、整體賦稅收入 
（一）實徵淨額 
10 月實徵淨額 5,669 億元，較上年同月增加 1,495 億元；累計 1 至
10 月實徵 4 兆 1,383 億元，較上年同期增加 8,499 億元(+25.8%)，
主要由證交稅、營所稅及營業稅所貢獻。隨新版財政收支劃分法實
施，營業稅劃歸中央政府比率由 61.2%降為 4.5%(獲配稅額隨之減
少 2,662 億元)，致中央政府稅收增加數 3,906 億元(+15.8%)，不及
地方政府增加數 4,671 億元(+68.5%)。 
（二）預算達成率 
累 計1 至 10 月 實 徵 淨 額 占 全 年 預 算 數 91.4% ， 其 中 中 央 政 府 為
92.8%，地方政府為87.7%；與全年預算數差距分別為3,913億元、
2,227億元、1,614億元。 
圖 1 賦稅實徵淨額及年增率

說明：115 年 5-7 月年增率跳動較大，係因 114 年所得稅結算申報及繳納期限延長至 6 月底所致。

### Page 2

二、主要稅目別變動
（一）10 月實徵淨額與上年同月比較，以營利事業所得稅增加 1,133 億元、
證券交易稅增加 220 億元及綜合所得稅增加 120 億元較多。
（二）累計 1 至 10 月實徵淨額與上年同期比較，以證券交易稅增加 3,297
億元、營利事業所得稅增加 2,986 億元及營業稅增加 1,272 億元較多。
圖 2 主要稅目別變化
(115 年 1-10 月)
1.營利事業所得稅
(1)10 月實徵淨額 4,144 億元，較上年同月增加 1,133 億元(+37.6%)，
主因暫繳稅款成長。
(2)累計 1 至 10 月實徵淨額 1 兆 4,172 億元，較上年同期增加 2,986
億元(+26.7%)，主因結算申報自繳、暫繳稅款俱增及延分期稅款
陸續入帳。
2.綜合所得稅
10 月實徵淨額 614 億元，較上年同月增加 120 億元(+24.3%)，主因
扣繳稅款增加所致；累計 1 至 10 月實徵淨額 8,894 億元，較上年同
期增加 910 億元(+11.4%)，主因結算申報自繳稅額增加，以及盈餘分

配、薪資所得等扣繳稅款均呈成長。
3.營業稅
(1)10月受進口代徵稅額增加及適用外銷零稅率營業人申請退稅增加
之交互影響，營業稅實徵淨額為負77億元，較上年同月(負146億元)
增加69億元。
(2)累 計1 至10 月 實 徵 淨額6,153 億 元 ， 較 上年 同 期 增 加1,272 億元
(+26.1%)，主因內需穩定，資通與視聽產品、電子零組件等進口
代徵稅額增加，惟外銷營業人申請退稅亦增，抵銷部分增幅。

### Page 3

3
4.貨物稅
10月實徵淨額73億元，較上年同月減少41億元(-35.6%)，累計1至10
月實徵淨額948億元，較上年同期減少248億元(-20.7%)，主因新購汽
機車及電動車之減免徵退稅增加，加以汽柴油減徵幅度擴大所致。
5.證券交易稅
(1)10 月及累計 1 至 10 月上市上櫃股票平均每日成交值為 1 兆 1,210
億元、1 兆 1,437 億元，分別較上年同期(6,267 億元、4,632 億元)
增 78.9%、1.5 倍。
(2)10 月證交稅實徵淨額 523 億元， 較上年同月增加 220 億元
(+72.6%)；累計 1 至 10 月實徵淨額 5,583 億元，較上年同期增加
3,297 億元(+1.4 倍)。
6.關稅
10 月實徵淨額 132 億元，較上年同月增加 7 億元(+6.0%)，累計 1 至
10 月實徵淨額 1,356 億元，較上年同期增加 88 億元(+6.9%)，均因機
械及電機設備、基本金屬、塑橡膠及其製品等進口稅額增加。
7.贈與稅
10 月實徵淨額 23 億元，較上年同月減少 11 億元(-31.9%)，主因大額

稅款案件減少；累計 1 至 10 月實徵淨額 234 億元，較上年同期增加
32 億元(+15.9%)，主因大額稅款案件增加。
8.土地增值稅
10 月實徵淨額 56 億元，較上年同月增加 3 億元(+5.9%)，主因大額
稅款案件增加；累計 1 至 10 月實徵淨額 560 億元，較上年同期減少
11 億元(-2.0%)，主因房地交易量及大額稅款案件減少。
9.使用牌照稅
10 月實徵淨額 6 億元，較上年同月減少 1 億元(-10.2%)，累計 1 至
10 月實徵淨額 676 億元，較上年同期減少 1 億元(-0.1%)，主因開徵
輛數減少，以及稅款入帳時間差異所致。
（三）前 10月主要稅目實徵淨額占全年預算數之比率：
1.大於 100%(已提前達成全年預算目標)者有遺產稅(123%)、贈與稅
(107%)、印花稅(106%)、房屋稅(105%)及營所稅(102%)。
2.低於 100%者有牌照稅(99%)、營業稅(94%)、綜所稅(93%)、契稅
(90%)、關稅(88%)、菸酒稅(81%)、證交稅(80%)、貨物稅(79%)、土

增稅(71%)及特銷稅(47%)等。

### Page 4

4
三、政府別賦稅收入概況
(一)中央政府
累計 1 至 10 月中央政府賦稅收入(不含中央統籌分配稅款)實徵淨
額 2 兆 8,579 億元(占全年預算數 92.8%)，較上年同期增加 3,906 億元
(+15.8%)，主因新版財政收支劃分法施行，營業稅劃歸中央政府比率
大幅調降，獲配稅額隨之減少 2,662 億元，惟證交稅、所得稅分別增加
3,297 億元、3,306 億元，互抵後呈現上升。
(二)地方政府
累計 1 至 10 月地方政府賦稅
收入(含中央統籌分配稅款)實
徵淨額 1 兆 1,487 億元(占全年
預算數 87.7%)，較上年同期增
加 4,671 億元(+68.5%)，主因
納入中央統籌分配之營業稅
增加 4,013 億元所致。
(三)中央特種基金
累計 1 至 10 月撥入中央特種基金之健康福利捐實徵淨額 220 億元，較
上年同期增加 4 億元(+1.7%)；房地合一課徵所得稅撥入住宅基金 144
億元，較上年同期減少 6 億元(-4.0%)；撥入長照基金 953 億元(其中 575

億元來自房地合一課徵所得稅、220 億元來自菸稅，158 億元來自遺贈
稅)，較上年同期增加 2 億元(+0.2%)。

顧
市場焦點
期中選舉
• 美國期中選舉後，市場擔憂民主黨可能放緩AI資料中心的建設並提高對AI的監管，促使科技股
面臨估值修正壓力。但從基本面觀察，2026年標普500企業獲利年增率預估已由7月的26.5%
大幅上修至36.1%，其中資訊科技產業預估成長64.5%，通訊服務亦有52.0%。大型科技企業
仍具備穩健現金流與持續投入AI基礎建設的能力，AI長期發展趨勢尚未改變，產業基本面不宜
因短期估值修正而過度看淡。
中國A股
• 短期來看A股反彈行情有機會延續，主因為美國期中選舉落幕、中國政策環境偏寬鬆，以及A
股估值與情緒指標落在歷史相對低檔，具備超跌反彈條件，政策面方面，人行可能進一步降息，
政策利率由1.25%下調至1%，搭配IPO超募資金回購、穩定資金加大ETF買入力道，有助於改

善市場流動性並提振投資信心。
石油/黃金
• 波斯灣國家能源出口替代方案已略見成效，不過由於替代路徑運輸成本較高，能源價格仍居
高不下，觀察過去幾週出口及價格變化，推測在美伊關係未有明確變化的情境下，國際油價
將在每桶100美元附近震盪。
• 央行購金需求維持高檔，但相較去年同期累計未進一步放大。在此情境下，短中期黃金價格
能否進一步突破的關鍵，可能取決於聯準會貨幣政策的調整空間。

兆豐銀行所做任何投資意見與市場分析結果，係依據資料製作當時情況進行判斷

回
顧
• 選民不滿情緒發酵，民主黨在期中選舉取得重大突破：伊朗局勢久拖不決、通膨持續攀升，削弱選民對川普政府的支持，民
主黨因此在期中選舉中於參、眾兩院取得過半席次，新一屆國會將在2027年1月3日就職，進入白宮與國會分由不同政黨掌
控的「分裂政府」時代。州長選戰方面，民主黨也成功翻轉多個原由共和黨長期執政的州，州長席次占比睽違20年再度超越
共和黨，其中德州、喬治亞州、俄亥俄州及愛荷華州的選情翻轉尤其受到矚目。
• 國會制衡與州政府版圖變化，政策走向仍存不確定性： 儘管民主黨成功奪回眾議院，但在參議院尚未取得足以單獨推動重大
法案的關鍵席次，例如除了財政預算相關法案可以用預算調節程序簡單多數通過外，一般的法案都需要60票才能通過，因此
未來重大政策仍須仰賴跨黨協商，市場對政策劇烈轉向的疑慮可望有所緩解。不過，民主黨掌握更多州政府，也可能促使部

分州在川普剩餘兩年任期內，進一步制衡或抵制聯邦施政議程，並對2028年總統大選的選務布局產生重要影響。
美國民主黨贏得聯邦參議院、眾議院以及德克薩斯州州長選舉

• 美國經濟具韌性，紮根美股未來底氣：美國期中選舉後，市場擔憂民主黨可能放
緩AI資料中心的建設並提高對AI的監管，促使科技股面臨估值修正壓力。但從基
本面觀察，標普500企業獲利展望仍持續改善。2026年標普500企業獲利年增率
預估已由7月的26.5%大幅上修至36.1%，其中資訊科技產業預估成長64.5%，通
訊服務亦有52.0%。短期而言，市場對AI資本支出效益及投資回收期的疑慮，可
能使科技股評價面臨重新檢視，並進一步影響輝達、半導體、伺服器及相關供應
鏈；然而，大型科技企業仍具備穩健現金流與持續投入AI基礎建設的能力，AI長
期發展趨勢尚未改變，產業基本面不宜因短期估值修正而過度看淡。

美國政治藍色浪潮來襲：AI數據中心建設受阻與產業鏈資產重估
• 政治監管與地方風向轉變：AI基礎設施擴建面臨停工與審查的壓力，民主黨在多個關鍵州分贏得州長選舉，並伴隨地方公投
限制措施的升溫，使得原本蓬勃發展的AI數據中心建設計畫大幅放緩。
• 關鍵州分政策轉嚴：德州、喬治亞州與俄亥俄州等AI數據中心投資重鎮，當選民主黨州長普遍對數據中心開發採取強硬立場。
德州喊出暫停新建並取消稅收優惠，喬治亞州提出全州暫緩新項目，俄亥俄州與威斯康辛州亦對能源消耗、環境審查及稅收
補貼提出嚴格管制條件。
• 地方公投與審查週期拉長：全美46項涉及數據中心的地方限制措施中，高達40項趨向收緊或已通過更嚴格限制，未來開發
項目將面臨更長審批週期、更嚴格電網接入審查及更高公用事業與監管合規成本。

• 未成熟開發商延期風險高：以全美儲備規模最大的德州為例，超過半數項目尚處於早期階段，且大量由缺乏營運經驗的初次
開發商主導。在監管與水電資源收緊下，此類項目極易面臨延期或取消的風險。
AI建設熱潮面臨民主黨藍色浪潮衝擊 46項地方數據中心措施投票結果分布
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.11.05

美國製造業景氣加速擴張，原物料成本壓力仍牽制通膨降溫
• 製造業擴張動能增強，生產與新訂單同步改善：美國10月ISM製造業PMI升至55.5，較9月54.5增加1%，連續第10個月
維持擴張；其中生產指數升至59.0，創2021年11月以來新高，新訂單指數亦升至55.5，顯示製造業接單及生產活動持
續增溫，且存貨指數回升至51.4，反映企業庫存由收縮轉為擴張，製造業景氣復甦動能進一步增強。
• 需求回升伴隨成本壓力，通膨降溫仍面臨挑戰：供應商交貨指數升至59.1，代表交貨速度持續放緩，連續11個月呈現相
同趨勢；客戶端庫存指數維持41.6，顯示庫存水位仍偏低，有利後續補庫存需求。然而，原物料價格指數雖由77.9降至
75.2，仍連續第25個月處於上漲區間，反映鋼鋁、石化及進口商品成本壓力尚未消退，可能使通膨降溫進程更加緩慢。

• 後市觀察：美國製造業景氣持續擴張，反映企業需求及經濟活動仍具韌性，有助支撐企業營收與獲利；惟原物料價格維
持高檔，可能增加通膨黏著性，使聯準會貨幣政策維持審慎，並對美債殖利率形成支撐。後續市場將關注製造業需求能
否延續，以及成本壓力是否逐步傳導至終端物價。
資料來源：Bloomberg， 2026.11.3

資料來源：ISM Manufacturing PMI Report， 2026.11.3

企業財務與行業分化：高槓桿新雲業者受創，巨頭發揮定價優勢
• 政策與審查趨嚴使得整體AI建設成本上揚，但在產業內卻呈現出極端的強者恆強、弱者面臨淘汰的兩極趨勢。
• 新雲公司(Neoclouds)面臨流動性與違約風險：若數據中心建設延誤或無法如期接入電網，收入推遲產生，而這類新興雲端
服務商普遍具高財務槓桿，高額利息費用持續累積，導致現金流期限錯配，將提高其再融資風險、擴大信用利差，甚至觸發
合約中的不可抗力條款與債務違約風險。
• 超大規模雲端巨頭(Hyperscalers)享受供應趨緊紅利：對科技巨頭而言，基礎設施擴建受阻反而是利多。已擁有現成數據中
心與電力保障的巨頭定價權大幅提升，算力供給緊俏能將雲端計算服務價格維持在高檔。此外，巨頭有能力自行籌資進行專
用發電或直接與公用事業公司談判，進一步拉開與新進入者的差距。

• AI六大支柱亮警報：支撐AI熱潮持續發展六大條件(訂單積壓、融資、算力需求、競爭、政治、聯準會)中，政治、競爭及聯
準會三項已現疲軟，特別是詞元(Token)成本持續下降、模型可替代性提高，低價模型加劇競爭，持續擠壓軟體獲利空間。
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.11.05
詞元(Token)成本持續下降• 小結與投資建議：期中選舉引發的政策變革，正將AI
行業從無差別大舉擴建帶入嚴格監管與高成本的新階
段。供給側瓶頸(電網、土地、監管)將淘汰財務體質孱
弱、高度依賴外部融資的高槓桿新興開發商；供給受
限也鞏固已具備規模與資源保障的雲端巨頭壟斷優勢
與定價權。 投資建議避開高槓桿新雲與中小型開發商，
聚焦資本雄厚且自備水電資源的超大規模雲端巨頭，
密切關注聯準會貨幣政策與信用利差變化，AI行業的
金融條件正在收緊，若聯準會降息放慢或信用利差持
續擴大，AI供應鏈中的高估值軟體與硬體股可能面臨
新一輪估值修正。

顧
AI資本支出持續攀升，現金流承壓加劇企業信用分化
• AI基礎建設投資擴大，企業融資需求同步升溫：AI競賽已由
模型及技術開發延伸至資料中心、運算晶片與電力基礎設施
建置，帶動大型科技企業持續提高資本支出。Microsoft、
Alphabet、Amazon等企業近期公布的財報均顯示，AI相關
投資已進入高強度建置階段，市場預估大型科技企業2026年
AI基礎建設投資將超過7,000億美元，龐大資金需求也使市場
更加關注企業融資成本、資本配置效率及未來現金流表現。
• 財報反映投資成本，現金流與信用評等成市場檢驗重點：Q3
財報顯示，Alphabet及Amazon均因AI基礎建設投資大幅增
加，使自由現金流承受壓力；其中Amazon近12個月自由現
金流轉為負值，Alphabet單季亦出現負自由現金流。相較之

下，Microsoft仍維持正向自由現金流，反映企業財務承受能
力有所差異。此外，Meta因AI資本支出規模擴大及未來現金
流不確定性，遭標普全球調降信用評等展望至負向，顯示市
場已開始將AI投資效益納入企業信用風險評估。
• 市場看法：AI長期需求仍具成長潛力，但市場評價將逐步由
資本支出規模轉向投資報酬率、自由現金流及財務槓桿管理。
而具備穩定營運現金流、較高資本回收效率及明確AI商業化
成果的企業，較有能力維持投資動能；反之，若資本支出持
續攀升而營收及獲利未能同步跟上，將增加企業融資需求與
信用風險，並使科技企業間的評價差異進一步擴大。
大型科技企業擴大資本支出，承諾規模持續攀升
AI產業表外承諾大幅揚升

中國10月製造業PMI跌回收縮區間，產需動能同步降溫
• 中國10月製造業PMI跌回收縮區間，產需動能同步降溫：中國10月製造業PMI降至49.3%，低於市場預期，也跌破50榮枯線，
顯示製造業景氣再度轉弱，主要受到十一長假前需求提前釋放，以及前期海外聖誕訂單集中出貨後進入調整期影響，使需求與
生產動能同步放緩，新訂單指數和生產指數皆下降，顯示企業生產活動亦有所收斂，不過若與去年同期相比，新訂單與生產指
數分別增加0.6及0.1個百分點，顯示製造業基本面仍具一定韌性，景氣表現優於去年同期，此外企業對未來仍維持審慎樂觀態
度，生產經營活動預期指數為53.4%，雖較前月略降，但仍位於擴張區間。
• 展望後市：隨著專項債加速投放，及8,000億元政策性金融工具逐步轉化為實體投資，基建及製造業需求有望持續釋放，帶動裝

備製造、鋼鐵等產業鏈景氣改善，此外11月為企業衝刺全年營運目標的重要時點，訂單接單與生產活動將較10月回溫，產業表
現方面，科技業因受惠於政策扶持及產業升級趨勢，維持強勁動能，消費品製造業則有望受雙十一購物帶動成長。

民主黨主張監管政策 間接支撐黃金價格表現
• 期中選舉為黃金價格帶來短期支撐：民主黨主導國會加深兩黨政治僵局的擔憂，加上可能推動更嚴格的AI產業監管，市場擔
憂對美國經濟成長帶來負面影響，進而增加聯準會採取寬鬆貨幣政策的可能性，同時美元走貶也是上週支撐金價的因素之一。
• 央行需求基本面穩健：世界黃金協會最新報告指出，各國央行在9月持續增加黃金持有量，當月淨購買量達61噸，為今年迄
今最高的單月總量。同時數據顯示，全球各國央行所申報的黃金淨購買量為228噸，較去年同期下降 7%。
• 觀點：央行購金需求維持高檔，但相較去年同期累計未進一步放大，同時高水準的美債殖利率排擠黃金的避險需求。在此情
境下，短中期黃金價格能否進一步突破的關鍵，可能取決於聯準會貨幣政策的調整空間。雖然市場大致排除激進升息的預期，

但緩步升息仍是基本情境，預料近期黃金價格將在每盎司4,000~4,200震盪。消息面來看，美伊衝突維持僵局，不過若是荷
姆茲海峽議題能有重大突破，將為黃金帶來進一步上漲空間。

期中選舉牽動，上週國際油價大幅震盪
• 選舉結果令國際油價大跌：選舉結果出爐後，市場預期民主黨將在預算與軍事決策上牽制總統川普，促使中東僵局加速透過
外交協商落幕。結果公布當天布蘭特油價單日大跌約9%，至每桶94美元，臨近週末則小幅反彈。上週油價整體收跌約16%，
主要反映選舉風險溢價消退，不過美伊僵局未變，油價仍在100美元附近震盪。
• 中東原油出口接近戰前水準，但成品油出口仍大幅落後：波斯灣國家正致力原油出口替代方案，目標擺脫荷姆茲海峽航運依
賴。石油出口量近幾週雖有所回落，但已接近戰前平均水準；然而，成品油出口動能恢復緩慢，目前仍遠不及戰前平均，這
也是造成全球能源通膨的主要原因。
• 觀點：波斯灣國家能源出口替代方案已略見成效，不過由於替代路徑運輸成本較高，能源價格仍居高不下，觀察過去幾週出

口及價格變化，推測在美伊關係未有明確變化的情境下，國際油價將在每桶100美元附近震盪。

資人申購基金係持有基金受益憑證，而非本資料提及之投資資產或標的。

付本金、利息或破產而蒙受虧損。部分基金不適合無法承擔相關風險之投資人。基金經理公司以往之經理績效不保證基金之最低投資收益；基金經理公司除盡善良

### Page 1

东方金诚宏观研究

1 
2026年 11月 LPR报价保持稳定，短期内下调可能性不大 
 王青  冯琳 
2026年 11月 20日，中国人民银行授权全国银行间同业拆借中心公布新版 LPR报价：1
年期品种报 3.0%，上月为 3.0%；5年期以上品种报 3.5%，上月为 3.5%。 
解读：

11月两个期限品种的 LPR报价保持不变，符合市场预期。11月初，央行优化短端利率调
控机制，宣布自 11月 2日起每日开展固定利率的隔夜逆回购操作招标，并明确目前操作利率
是 1.25%；7天期逆回购操作调整为多重价位中标，政策利率属性淡化。这意味着，央行主要
政策利率从 7天期向隔夜“换锚”。“换锚”后，DR001 的走廊区间从 1.15%-1.65%下调至
1.0%-1.5%。但需要指出的是，这并非政策利率下调，也意味着 11月 LPR报价的定价基础没
有变化。另外，尽管近期 DR001 等主要货币市场利率、银行同业存单到期收益率、存款利率
都有不同程度的下行，但受贷款利率持续走低等影响，当前银行净息差仍然处在历史低位附
近，报价行缺乏主动下调 LPR报价加点的动力。 
近期 LPR报价保持稳定，根本原因是三季度 GDP增速回升到 4.5%，出口延续高增，以高
技术制造业为代表的新质生产力领域加速发展；10 月宏观数据有所走弱，但更多属于月度正
常波动，四季度经济增长动能仍有上行趋势，完成全年“4.5%至 5.0%”的经济增长目标难度
不大。在此背景下，近期包括全面降息在内的总量货币政策保持定力。 
展望未来，短期内 LPR报价下调的可能性不大。主要原因在于，9月 29日央行调整完善
若干结构性货币政策工具，包括 PSL 利率下调 0.25 个百分点，支农支小再贷款额度上调
5000亿，其中民企再贷款额度上调 3000亿，科技创新和技术改造再贷款额度上调 2000亿，
“报销”比例由原来的 60%上调至 100%；与此同时，财政部门也会同央行、金融监管总局，
首次在全国范围内出台了针对部分首套刚需购房贷款的贴息政策。这意味着逆周期调节已优
先选择结构性政策工具发力。我们判断，在以上结构性稳增长、稳楼市措施出台后，接下来
将进入一段政策效果观察期，短期内全面降息等总量货币政策工具跟进的可能性下降，LPR
报价的定价基础会保持稳定。 
拉长时间来看，综合考虑经济金融演化趋势，预计 2027年央行有望全面降息 10到 20个

基点（0.1到 0.2个百分点），届时将引导 LPR报价跟进下调。

### Page 1

M
Idea
Alchip Technologies Ltd  | Asia Pacific
European NDR: strong five-year
outlook in AI ASICs; OW
Alchip's strong growth potential is rooted in US CSPs' AI ASIC
replacement cycle. Even after the recent rally, our price target
still implies 46% upside.
Right after its  3Q26 earnings call, we took Alchip's CEO, Mr. Johnny Shen, and CFO,
Mr. Daniel Wang, to visit European investors for discussion of the company’s long-
term strategy and outlook. Here are key takeaways:

Entering another phase of fast growth in the coming 4-5 years, with good
profitability: Alchip is sticking with its back-end design service focus, which
dovetails with CSPs' strategy of COT (customer-owned tooling). The long-term
trend is for CSP and AI lab customers to own the architecture and front-end design,
while pursuing efficiency in back-end design and production service. That strategy
can maximize the tokens generated per dollar  of AI capex. Besides winning future

generations with a major customer (AWS, 2nm and 1.4nm), Alchip has secured a
second big customer and is very close to winning a project with another US CSP (we
think it should be Google –  TPU computing dies). We project US$10bn total revenue
in 2028 –  4x our US$2.4bn estimate for  2026. Management thinks US$20bn
revenue p.a. by the end of this decade is possible, if the balance sheet permits.
More discussion of the second customer: The company still cannot disclose the

name of the second customer given the NDA, but reiterated that it expects a US$1bn
revenue contribution in 2027. Alchip provides part of the design service for the end
customer, and the gross margin is reasonably good. Although we don’t rule out the
possibility of another design service provider in this ASIC project for IP or front-end
design, we maintain our view that the end customer is a US CSP that uses Alchip for
back-end design service. Our industry checks don't see a CoWoS booking for this

ASIC, so we think it could be related to AI networking.
Getting close to winning a third big customer: In our October 11 report, Solid 2027
and 2028 outlook with potential catalysts, we already indicated that Alchip is a
strong candidate for Google TPU computing die back-end design service.
Management continued to show high confidence in a new project win without
specifying customer name; it expects some NRE revenue to come by the end of
2027. We asked which customer (second or third) could make a bigger revenue

contribution 2-3 years down the road – management thinks the third customer may
show bigger revenue potential.
Alchip's value underpinned by major customer (AWS): This could be reflected in
higher allocation of Trainium3 in 2027 and better business terms for Trainium4
(profits to grow with the volume upside). Management also expects 1.4nm design
activities to kick off some time in 2027 –  the key to five-year growth visibility.
Please see the following section for investors' feedback and our views.

### Page 2

Idea
2
Stronger revenue   guidance for 2027 and 2028
European investors' feedback
1. Alchip was somewhat overlooked in the AI ASIC market over the past two years,
compared with MediaTek and GUC, which continued to win new projects. Winning
second and third big customers is considered a re-rating driver – it mitigates the
concentration in a single customer (AWS).
2.  Meanwhile, European investors generally agree that AI ASICs should gain share
against GPUs, given tighter AI capex budgets. We share the view expressed in

recent comments from Alchip and MediaTek management: certain AI ASICs that
companies help to design provide very competitive TCO (total cost of ownership).
3. It is hard to track ASIC supply chain data points in real time, but the ASIC story
and Alchip's industry position can justify a long-term investment. Changes in
short-term data points wouldn't stop European investors from investing in Alchip.
4.  On valuation, investors think Alchip's 2028e implied P/E of 11x based on our EPS

estimate is relatively attractive vs. other global ASIC design service peers.
Considering this feedback, we think Alchip stock should  continue to re-rate: The stock
has been volatile, rebounding 50% since November 6 after a 40% pullback over October
12 to November  6. (The TAIEX was  +10% and -20% over those periods.) Based on our
estimates, P/E is now 17x for 2027 and 11x for 2028, lower than its historical average of
28x one-year forward P/E since 2019.

Key catalysts could be:
• November 30 –  AWS re:Invent conference with Trainium4 announcement;
• Supply chain identification of the second big customer's name and order potential;
• Confirmation of Google computing die back-end design service;
• Bigger Trainium3 revenue ramp in 3Q27 vs. 2Q27.

MS Rating
▪ We continue to believe that AI ASICs will

outgrow AI GPUs in the coming years, and
Alchip is one of the few ASIC design service
providers that can deal with increasingly
complex designs (at 2nm) with good
production/design track records for large
leading edge chips.
▪ AWS's announcement of its 3nm
Trainium3 chip matches the timing of
Alchip's 3nm project win. The growing
number of use cases for Trainium (e.g.,
Apple's adoption) bodes well for Alchip's
revenue growth in 2026.
▪ We believe Alchip has strong potential to

win Trainium4.
▪ Our price target implies 26x our 2027e
EPS, which we ﬁnd undemanding vs. our
>100% revenue CAGR for 2025-28e.

NT$6,880.00
30x 2027e EPS
AI chipsets and HPC projects start to
proliferate, amid substantial end demand.

Alchip's leading-edge tape-out numbers
increase signiﬁcantly, with some high-proﬁle
project wins at 3nm or even 2nm. Alchip is
able to secure enough foundry capacity
(front-end wafers and back-end packaging)
and substrates for all of its leading-edge
turnkey production. Alchip gradually gains
market share within the design service
market, amid continued proliferation of
custom chip demand.
NT$5,888.00
26x 2027e EPS
AI chipsets and HPC projects start to

proliferate amid strong end demand,
resulting in steady growth in tape-outs and
volume. There might be a slowdown in
Alchip's Chinese customers' design activity
for leading-edge chips as a result of US
export controls; we believe this would be
largely offset by ASIC demand strength
from non-China regions.
NT$2,465.00
11x 2027e EPS
AI chipsets and HPC projects lack concrete
end demand, resulting in delayed tape-outs
and mass production. Alchip's leading-edge

project pipeline deteriorates, or some
customers turn directly to TSMC. AI GPU's
dominance in AI computing compresses the
market share gain potential for overall ASIC,
with customers starting to slow their
custom silicon developments - and therefore
ASIC procurement plans.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

Operating margin reached 38.6% (+4.1pp QoQ), 1.4/3.2pp above 
our/consensus forecasts. Management guided for 4Q26E/2027E revenue 
growth of 5-10% QoQ/50% YoY, with gross margin of c.62%/above 60%, 
respectively. We believe the company’s guidance is conservative and 
forecast 4Q26/2027 revenue to be TWD6.7bn/TWD37.7bn, with gross 
margin of 61.8%/61.5% and operating margin of 39.5%/39.9%, 
respectively.

What’s the impact: Probe card capacity expansion on track. MPI 
maintained its capacity expansion plan, targeting monthly VPC/MEMS 
capacities of 2.0m/3.5m probes by end-2026 and 2.5m/6.0m by end-2027. 
Management plans to release its final 2027 expansion plan by end-
December, with potential upside to current targets. Demand remains solid, 
with capacity still insufficient to meet customer needs and no signs of order 
cuts, per management. We believe testing intensity is the key driver of 
sector growth and we expect MPI's diversified customer base to limit the 
impact of order fluctuations from any single customer or project.

CPO contribution likely to accelerate in 2028. Although the market 
seems conservative about its CPO ramp-up, the company claimed that 
Insertion 2 qualification at a tier-1 customer remains on track, with 
qualification results expected by end-December and potential mass 
production orders in 1H27. For Insertion 3, qualification is progressing with 
multiple customers, with potential production orders in 1H27. Given 
qualification lead times, we forecast the CPO testing machine to contribute 
5-13% of revenue over 2027-28, aligned with management’s expectation 
that CPO will contribute a double-digit % in 2028.

What we recommend: We lower our 2026-28E EPS by 6-13% to reflect 
higher opex and cut our 4-quarter-forward target PER multiple from 65x to 
45x to reflect recent macro uncertainties, peer derating and moderating 
earnings growth. As a result, we lower our 12-month TP to TWD5,625 (from 
TWD8,415), while reaffirming our Buy (1) rating. Our target multiple implies 
a c.0.6 PEG based on our 2026-28E EPS CAGR of 73%. Given its 
diversified customer base and continued AI/HPC demand growth, we view 
current levels as offering an attractive risk-reward profile.

How we differ: Our 2027-28E EPS are 4-5% below the Bloomberg 
consensus, likely on our more conservative opex assumptions.

16 November 2026

Solid fundamentals; attractive entry post derating

➢ Capacity expansion on track; AI/HPC demand remains intact 
➢ CPO contribution likely to become more significant in 2028  
➢ Reaffirming our Buy (1) rating; lowering 12-month TP to TWD5,625

Company profile 
Founded in Hsinchu in 1995, MPI is a leading global probe card supplier offering CPC, VPC and MEMS 
solutions, alongside photonics, semiconductor and thermal testing equipment. Growth is driven by 
expanding AI/HPC demand, rising MEMS adoption, capacity expansion, deeper vertical integration and 
emerging opportunities in CPO testing.

### Page 1

Taiwan Information Technology

What’s new: Following on our results Memo for Andes, we widen our 
2026E net loss and maintain our Hold (3) rating. Management aims for a 
profitable 4Q26 to cover YTD losses, yet we are unsure how sustainable its 
bottom-line turnaround will be in the near term, given still uncertain timing 
of royalty conversion despite enriched IP and customer portfolios. That 
said, we remain structurally positive on Andes’s outlook, thanks to its 
leading position in the RISC-V architecture well prepared to capitalise on 
the AI cycle from cloud to edge to physical.

What’s the impact: Forecast changes in a mixed bag. We widen our 
2026E net loss for Andes to factor in its higher-than-expected opex YTD, 
but we keep our 2027E EPS largely unchanged to factor in its frugal opex 
control starting from 3Q26. Though bottom-line turnaround looks still 
bumpy in the near term on its suboptimal operating leverage due to still 
suboptimal scale, as a result of still uncertain timing of royalty revenue 
conversion, we merit management’s opex control to speed up bottom-line 
turnaround. This, together with its enriched RISC-V IP portfolio and 
broadened customer base for market-share gain, should help sustain its 
turnaround from 2H27 when topline expands to improve earnings quality 
and investor confidence.

Patience still for a sustainable turnaround. Despite this year’s business 
hiccups on delayed royalty ramp and opex volatility, we remain positive on 
Andes’s structural outlook, given its leading position in the RISC-V 
architecture offering enriched high-performance/low-power compute IP as 
substitutes to Arm and x86 CPU counterparts to capitalise on the secular AI 
cycle, with a broad coverage of applications from accelerated compute and 
embedded control to automotive. Management’s comments support our 
view, as it expects the RISC-V’s open-source ecosystem to drive innovation 
allowing designs of instruction sets tailored to customers’ specific needs, 
which should help enhance customer stickiness while attracting new 
customers for market-share gains. That said, patience may be still needed 
for Andes’s monetisation to bear fruit for a sustainable turnaround.

What we recommend: Despite our forecast revisions, we raise our 12M 
TP to TWD240 (from TWD180), still based on DCF methodology (WACC: 
18%, terminal growth: 3%) which we believe is appropriate for a startup IP 
company such as Andes. Given the still bumpy road to any sustainable 
turnaround, we maintain our Hold (3) call with key upside/downside risks of 
any faster RISC-V ecosystem build/further delays of royalty ramp.

How we differ: Our 2027-28E EPS are 29-48% above the consensus 
forecast likely due to our lower opex assumptions for Andes.

17 November 2026

Near-term turnaround still looks bumpy

➢ 2026E net loss likely to widen on opex volatility but … 
➢ … no harm to structural turnaround from 2H27 
➢ Maintaining our Hold (3) rating with a higher 12M TP of TWD240

Company profile 
Founded in 2005, Andes is an embedded microprocessor intellectual property (IP) provider with 
solutions in high-performance and low-power 32/64-bit processors and associated SoC platforms. Its 
solutions can be adopted for diverse applications such as IoT, 5G, AI, Bluetooth/WiFi devices, gaming 
and automotive. As a founding member of RISC-V International, Andes focuses on developing 
processor IPs based on the RISC-V instruction-set architecture while its mature V3 architecture is the 
main contributor of its royalty revenue.

### Page 5

5

Andes Technology (6533 TT): 17 November 2026 
ESG analysis

Update Date: 17 Nov 2026 
Source: Daiwa, Company

ESG risks
Risks Management Analyst comments
G
Executive/board
quality 1
Andes’ Board of Directors consists of seven members, including three independent directors,
representing approximately 43% of the total board composition, exceeding the regulatory
minimum requirement of 20%. The roles of Chairman and President are held by separate
individuals, reflecting a clear division of leadership responsibilities.
Currently, the Board includes one female director among the seven seats. Andes acknowledges
that gender diversity at the board level remains an area for improvement and is actively
committed to strengthening diversity and inclusion as part of its ongoing governance
enhancement efforts.
Capital management 1
As a semiconductor IP provider without physical manufacturing operations, Andes does not
require significant capital expenditure for factories. Instead, its primary capital needs are related
to ongoing investment in research and development to support new IP development and capture
emerging market opportunities. From this perspective, we believe Andes is inclined to retain a
portion of its earnings to fund future IP innovation rather than fully distributing profits to
shareholders.
Andes did not distribute dividends prior to 2019. In 2019, it paid out 72% of its 2018 net profit as
dividends. Subsequently, the dividend payout ratio remained high at approximately 90% during
the period from 2020 to 2022. However, the payout ratio declined sharply to 21% in 2023 based
on 2022 net profit, reflecting a more conservative capital allocation approach.
Due to weakened profitability, Andes did not distribute dividends in 2024 or 2025, as earnings
were insufficient to support shareholder payouts. Management has prioritized financial flexibility
and the preservation of resources to navigate challenging market conditions and sustain long-
term R&D investment.
Related party &
transaction 1
Before July 2021, Andes conducted related party transactions with MediaTek (2454 TT) given
each company's chairman was the same person. MediaTek was Andes' largest customer with a
31% revenue contribution in 2013. Its revenue contribution has gradually declined in past years
to a single digit percentage in 2021. Since Andes' board of directors' election in July 2021, its
chairman and MediaTek's chairman are no longer the same person. Andes currently does not
have any meaningful related party transactions.
S Supply chain management 1
As a semiconductor IP provider without physical products, Andes mainly procures softwares &
services from its suppliers. Andes implements supplier management policies to ensure that there
is no violation of corporate social responsibility policy in the procurement contract with its
suppliers.
E Water & wastewater management 1
Andes is a semiconductor IP provider without any physical products. Thus, it does not have on-
site manufacturing activities to weigh on the environment. It has set up a goal to reduce water
consumption by 2% every year through measures such as installing faucet water savers and
promoting the practice of saving water.
E
Waste & hazardous
materials
management
1 Andes is a semiconductor IP provider without any physical products. Thus, it does not have on- site manufacturing activities to weigh on the environment.
E GHG emissions 1
Andes is a semiconductor IP provider without any physical products. Thus, it does not have on-
site manufacturing activities to weigh on the environment. However, the company still applies
measures to reduce power consumption for its operations, such as turning off lights one hour per
day during the lunch break. It has set up a goal to reduce its total electricity consumption by 1-
2% every year.
Note: Management score represents a company's ability to manage/benefit from certain ESG topics. The scores range from 1 to 3, with 1 being the strongest.

Idea
Delta Electronics Inc.  | Asia Pacific
Momentum to Pick Up in
Coming Quarters
We expect revenue to grow in  double digits (%) QoQ in both
4Q26 and 1Q27.  Low market expectations for HVDC in the 2H27
GPU platform skew risk/reward more  to the upside.
Revenue momentum picking up with better component supply: With October
monthly sales of NT$$74,666mn (+14% MoM/+30% YoY), we think Delta is on
tracking for 10-15% top-line growth QoQ for 4Q26, as component supply increases

and some power rack shipments  emerge, albeit with a relatively small contribution.
We expect the momentum to be sustained into 1Q27, likely another double-digit (%)
QoQ rise on the top line, along with the new GPU server ramp and postponed
shipments from past quarters. We anticipate  strong revenue growth in 2027, as
power consumption will increase significantly in the new GPU server racks. The
contributions from liquid cooling solutions and DC/DC modules will also be good

drivers.
HVDC power rack timing key to watch, but expectations not high: We expect
Delta to start shipping HVDC power racks to its customers from 4Q26, though the
contribution might be a low-single-digit (%) share of total revenue through 1H27, by
our estimate. All eyes are on the adoption of HVDC power racks in the next-
generation AI server platforms – we think power consumption at the rack level is at
the threshold of HVDC power racks being a necessity. Our checks indicate that most

supply chain players now see HVDC as the reference design for the GPU server
generation scheduled for 2H27. Though this does not appear finalized yet, the
chances of that happening should increase  moving closer to the launch. Moreover,
we believe that the base case for most people is for HVDC not to be the only power
delivery structure in that 2H27 GPU server platform, which points to potential
upside in 2H27/2028.
Liquid cooling and fuel cell good complementary businesses: Delta has been a

dominant supplier in liquid cooling solutions, which should show adoption
proliferating from computing/switch trays to more devices, including power shelves,
optical transceivers, etc. For the fuel cell business, we believe Delta's expertise in
power delivery could help it offer more integrated solutions. Smaller-scale projects
should start from 2027 and could grow into a much more meaningful revenue
stream. The board  approved a greenfield project in Guanyin (Taiwan) with  capex of

~NT$10bn back in May 2026. We expect fuel cells to reach a multi-billion dollar (US
$) revenue scale in the coming few years. .
Stay OW: Current valuation is 16x 2027e P/E vs. the trough level of 14x. We view
risk-reward as attractive, given Delta's dominant position in AI power/cooling related
offerings. We stay OW on the stock and believe that accelerating momentum in the
near term should help it outperform. Our NT$2,300 price target is based on 30x

the 2027 Extel Asia Research Survey.

Idea
4
Valuation Methodology
Our price target (base case scenario value) is NT$2,300, derived from 30x average
2027-2028e EPS: We use price/earnings as our valuation methodology because we
believe it appropriately captures Delta's earnings growth potential. We believe our target
P/E multiple of 30x is justified by an earnings CAGR of 59% over 2025-28e, which implies
a PEG of 0.5. This is lower than the average of 1.0 in 2022-25, when the stock was trading

at an average P/E of 23x for a 23% earnings CAGR.
Bull case scenario value – NT$3,050: We apply a 40x P/E multiple   to our average base
case estimate for 2027-28. This scenario assumes a greater-than-expected contribution
from higher-margin businesses such as power electronics and liquid cooling as well as
greater synergies from acquired companies.
Bear case scenario value – NT$920 (from NT$1,050):  We apply a 12x P/E multiple  (from
14x) to our average base case estimate for 2027-28. This scenario assumes further profit

drag from legacy products, smaller profit contribution from higher-margin businesses,
higher opex and/or less meaningful contribution from acquired companies.

Risk Reward - Delta Electronics Inc. (2308.TW)

ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

旺矽成立於 1995 年，為全球前五大探針卡
廠，主要從事半導體測試板的設計與生產，
品項包括晶圓測試(CP)的懸臂式探針卡、垂
直式探針卡、微機電探針卡、探針卡 PCB、
中介層載板等；半導體設備製造，包含光電
自動化設備、溫度測試設備、先進半導體測
試設備。3Q26 產品比重：探針卡 72.7%、
半導體設備 25.4%、其他業務 1.9%。

2027 年起量，2028 年有望大量認列營收。˙7 
 投資評價與建議 
建議買進：3Q26 毛利率優於預期，研究部上修 2026 年獲利預估值，惟 CPO 設
備營收大量認列時點晚於先前預期，研究部下修 2027/2028 年 CPO 設備營收預
估值，並同步下調 2027/2028 EPS 預估值，預估 2026/2027/2028 年 EPS 由
70.85/164.37/245.20 元調整至 71.29/137.63/242.83 元，由於近期 AI 雜音相對
較多，研究部同步調整 PE 乘數，目標價由 9805 元(2028EPS*40X)下修至 7285
元(2028 EPS*30X)，維持買進投資建議。

公司簡介：旺矽成立於 1995 年，為全球前五大探針卡廠，主要從事半導體測試板
的設計與生產，品項包括晶圓測試(CP)的懸臂式探針卡（Cantilever Probe Cards, 
CPC）、垂直式探針卡（Vertical Probe Cards, VPC）、微機電探針卡(Micro Electro 
Mechanical Systems, MEMS)、探針卡 PCB、中介層載板(interposer/substrate)
等；半導體設備製造，包含光電自動化設備(Photonics Automation, PA)、溫度測
試設備(Thermal Test, Thermal) 、先進半導體測試設備(Advanced 
Semiconductor Test, AST)。其他業務包含各子公司營收，長洛國際(銷售母公司
旺矽機台設備)、琉明光電(LED 晶粒代工)、Celadon Systems(2021 年 8 月併購，
生產 AST 工程測試探針卡)。客戶包含全球各大 IC 設計公司、晶圓代工廠、封測
廠。3Q26 產品比重：探針卡 72.7%、半導體設備 25.4%、其他業務 1.9%。 
3Q26 毛利率優於預期：旺矽 3Q26 營收 59.04 億元(+12.8%QoQ，+72.9%YoY)，
毛利率 61.7%，優於研究部預期 61.2%，主要受惠於 HPC 探針卡營收成長強勁，
產品組合轉佳，營益率 38.6%，稅後淨利 18.80 億元(+23.3%QoQ，+114.4%YoY)，
稅後 EPS 19.18 元。 
4Q26 營收維持季增趨勢：展望 4Q26，公司預期營收有望季增 5~10%，惟考量
AI ASIC 探針卡客戶持續放量出貨、Non-AI ASIC 探針卡客戶於 4Q26 開始貢獻，
以及半導體設備營收傾向年底認列，加上匯率維持有利，研究部預期營運表現有望
優於公司展望，預估 4Q26 營收 69.90 億元（+18.4%QoQ，+82.2%YoY），毛利
率 62.1%，營益率 40.7%，稅後淨利 23.54 億元（+25.2%QoQ，+148.4%YoY），

2026/2027/2028 年 153.6/289.1/420.0 億元(+70.9%/+88.3%/+45.3%YoY)；
CPO 測試設備部分，I3 工程機客戶持續驗證中，I2 第一家客戶預期 2026 年底有

會有初步結果，CPO 測試設備將為 2027、2028 年設備業務主要成長動能，惟公
司表示預期 CPO 設備營收認列時間點落在 2028 年，研究部調整半導體設備營收

(+22.4%/+55.6%/+160.1%YoY)。

產業速報  
                                        2026 年 11 月 23 日

1 群益投顧 
旺矽(6223 TT)AI 晶片測試 2027 續旺。旺矽估 2027 年營收成長 50%以上，毛利率維持 60%以上；
2028 年 CPO 設備有望量產，將帶動評價提升，給 Trading Buy 的投資建議。

研 究 員 ： 莊 禮 誠   
dan.chuang@cim.capital.com.tw

旺矽 3Q26 毛 利 率 季 增 ：  
旺矽 3Q26 毛 利 率 達 61.7％ ， 較 2Q26 的 58.4％提升 3.3 個 pts， 單 季 稅 後 純
益 18.80 億 元 ， 季 增 23％ 、 年 增 114％ ， 每 股 稅 後 純 益 19.18 元 ， 顯 示 高 階
產 品 需 求 放 量 ， 帶 動 獲 利 能 力 顯 著 改 善 。 旺 矽 3Q26 毛 利 率 突 破 60%， 主 要
受惠 AI 及 HPC 相 關 產 品 出 貨 比 重 提 高 。  
旺 矽 估 4Q26 營 收 季 增 ：  
AI 及 高 效 能 運 算 (HPC)晶 片 測 試 需 求 持 續 升 溫，帶 動 測 試 介 面 大 廠 旺 矽 營 運
動 能 走 強。隨 先 進 製 程 晶 片 複 雜 度 提 高、測 試 接 點 數 持 續 增 加，旺 矽 高 階 探
針 卡 需 求 維 持 強 勁，旺矽估 4Q26 營 收 可 望 季 增 5％至 10％，4Q26、2027 全
年 毛 利 率 有 機 會 維 持 60%以 上 水 準 ， 營 運 成 長 動 能 延 續 至 2027 年。 
旺 矽 估 4Q26 毛 利 率 與 3Q26 相當：  
隨 AI 晶 片 持 朝 更 高 運 算 效 能 、 更 大 晶 片 面 積 及 先 進 封 裝 架 構 發 展 ， 測 試 接
點 數 同 步 攀 升，對 探 針 卡 精 密 度、可 靠 度 及 測 試 效 率 的 要 求 也 愈 來 愈 高。旺
矽 憑 藉 高 階 測 試 技 術 及 客 戶 認 證 優 勢 ， 有 利 擴 大 先 進 晶 片 測 試 市 場 布 局 。  
值得注意的是，隨高階探針卡技術門檻提高，相關產品製造成本也同步增
加，旺 矽 具 備 將 部 分 成 本 轉 嫁 客 戶 的 能 力，有 助 降 低 成 本 上 升 對 毛 利 率 的 衝
擊 。 加 上 高 附 加 價 值 產 品 出 貨 增 加 ， 4Q26 毛 利 率 仍 有 機 會 維 持 在 60%出頭
水準。 
CPO 設 備 有 望 於 2027 年 拿 到 訂 單 ， 2028 年 貢 獻 顯著營收：  
在 新 技 術 布 局 方 面 ， 共 同 封 裝 光 學 (CPO) 被 視 為 旺 矽 下 一 階 段 重 要 成 長 機
會。隨 AI 資 料 中 心 對 高 速 傳 輸 及 低 功 耗 需 求 提 升，光 電 整 合 技 術 逐 步 導 入，
將 進 一 步 提 高 相 關 晶 片 與 模 組 測 試 複 雜 度，為 測 試 介 面 供 應 商 創 造 新 商 機。 
目前旺矽 CPO 相 關 測 試 方 案 持 續 推 進 客 戶 驗 證 ， 其 中 第 三 階 段 (Insertion 3)
已 向 多 家 客 戶 交 付 工 程 測 試 設 備 ， 預 期 1H27 有 機 會 陸 續 取 得 量 產 設 備 訂
單；第 二 階 段 (Insertion 2) 預計 12/2026 取 得 首 家 客 戶 認 證 結 果，後 續 仍 須 視
客 戶 驗 證 及 導 入 進 度 而 定 。  
旺 矽 持 續 擴 充 探 針 卡 產 能 ：  
此 外 ， 旺 矽 2027 年 擴 產 規 劃 也 是 市 場 關 注 焦 點 。 隨 AI 及 HPC 需 求 延 續 ，
高階探針卡產能配置將攸關後續訂單承接能力，公司預期在年度預算規劃
完成後，2026 年 底 前 可 望 進 一 步 釐 清 2027 年 產 能 擴 充 方 向 。  
投 資 建 議 ：  
旺矽估 2027 年 營 收 成 長 50%以 上 ， 毛 利 率 維 持 60%以 上 ； 2028 年 CPO 設
備 有 望 量 產 ， 將 帶 動 評 價 提 升 ， 給 Trading Buy 的 投 資 建 議 。

3 群益投顧 
  【 投 資 評 等 說 明 】  
  評等 定義 
  強力買進(Strong Buy)  首 次 評 等 潛 在 上 漲 空 間 ≥ 35%  
  買進(Buy)  15%≤首 次 評 等 潛 在 上 漲 空 間 <35%  
  區間操作(Trading Buy) 5%≤首 次 評 等 潛 在 上 漲 空 間 <15%

中立(Neutral)  
無 法 由 基 本 面 給 予 投 資 評 等  
預 期 近 期 股 價 將 處 於 盤 整  
建 議 降 低 持 股

【 投 資 評 等 調 整 機 制 】  
  投 資 評 等 係「 首 次 」給 予 特 定 個 股 投 資 評 等 時，其 前 一 日 收 盤 價 相 較 3 個月
目 標 價 之 潛 在 上 漲 空 間 計 算 而 得 。 個 股 投 資 評 等 分 為 四 個 等 級 ， 定 義 如 上 。
爾 後 的 投 資 評 等 係 依 循 「 首 次 評 等 」 ， 直 到 停 止 推 薦 。   
「 強 力 買 進 」、「 買 進 」 及 「 區 間 操 作 」 均有 upside 目 標 價 。 差 別 僅 在 於 ，
首 次 評 等 時 潛 在 報 酬 率 不 同 。 「 中 立 」 則 無 目 標 價 。  
一 旦 我 們 給 予 特 定 個 股「 強 力 買 進 」、「 買 進 」或「 區 間 操 作 」之 投 資 評 等，
就 是 責 任 的 開 始，爾 後 將 透 過 各 式 研 究 報 告 作 定 期 性、持 續 性 追 蹤 基 本 面 及
股 價 變 化 ， 直 到 停 止 推 薦 。  
停 止 推 薦 情 境 ：  
1. 達 目 標 價 。  
2. 雖 未 達 原 訂 目 標 價 ， 但 檢 視 基 本 面 、 訊 息 面 、 籌 碼 面 等 多 方 訊 息 ， 研
判 股 價 上 漲 空 間 已 然 有 限 ， 將 適 時 出 具 降 評 報 告 。  
3. 推 薦 後 股 價 不 漲 反 跌 。

【免責聲明】 
  本 研 究 報 告 僅 提 供 予 特 定 人 之 客 戶 作 為 參 考 資 料「 非 經 同 意 不 得 轉 載 」。我
們 並 不 確 保 此 資 訊 的 完 整 性 與 正 確 性，投 資 人 應 了 解，報 告 中 有 關 未 來 預 測
之 陳 述 可 能 不 會 實 現，因 而 不 應 被 依 賴。而 且 此 報 告 並 非 根 據 特 定 投 資 目 的
或 依 預 定 對 象 之 財 務 狀 況 所 撰 寫 出 來 的，因 此，此 研 究 報 告 的 目 的，既 非 對
投 資 人 於 買 賣 證 券、選 擇 權、期 貨 或 其 他 證 券 相 關 之 衍 生 性 商 品 提 供 詢 價 服
務，亦 非 作 為 進 行 交 易 的 要 約。投 資 人 應 注 意 到 相 關 證 券 之 價 值 及 收 益，可
能會有無預警地上升或下降，產生投資回報金額可能比原始投資來得少的
情形。

3Q26 OP 2.7조, 당사 추정치 35% 상회 
서프라이즈의 근거가 판매보증비 감소에서 발생. 3Q26 기말환율의 낙폭 (-12% qoq)으로 
인한 역설적 비용절감의 규모가 전년동기 대비 1.2조 원을 넘어서며 환율의 조사모삼 효과
로 만들어진 컨센서스 상회 실적. 
긍정적인 부분: 컨틴전시, 신차 효과, 인센티브, 생산 정상화 
지속적인 컨틴전시 체제를 유지하며 비용절감 실천 (=지속된 요소). 4분기 추가적으로 나아
질 요인은 1) 내수 신차효과로 판가 상승 및 유기적 수익성 개선, 2) 공장 가동률 정상화를 
통한 고정비 부담 절감, 3) 해외시장 신차효과를 통한 딜러 인센티브 절감. 
경계할 부분: 재료비, 환율, BEV 판매비중 확대 
재료비 측면에서 1) 2Q26 시작된 미국-이란 전쟁 관련 원재료비 상승은 4Q26F에도 하락
세를 보이지 못할 것이며, 2) 구리, 철강 등의 금속 원자재의 가격 상승 또한 4Q26F 지속될 
것. 또한 3Q26과 반대로 qoq 기말환율 대비 낙폭 확대할 평균환율 (mtd 1,338원) 효과로 
환 관련 감익 본격화, IONIQ 3의 출시는 인센티브 절감인 동시에 믹스 관점에서 부정적. 
투자의견 BUY 유지, 목표주가 440,000원으로 소폭 상향 
목표주가 상향 근거는 컨틴전시 체제 유지를 통한 FY27F 자동차사업 부문 이익 예상치 상
향. 부정적 외생변수 (환율, 재료비)가 지속된다는 전제 하에 FY27F OPM 하락을 예상하나, 
(FY26F 5.6%, FY27F 5.2%) 향후 신차효과와 생산 정상화에 따른 자동차 사업부문 수익성 
반등의 여력 존재. 주가 상승 트리거는 1) CES 전후 BD 관련 기대감, 2) 신차 출시 이후 견
조한 판매량, 3) 환율, 원재료비 등 외생변수의 개선.

### Page 1

한화에어로스페이스 (012450)

EARNINGS REVIEW Company Analysis | 방위산업 | 2026. 11. 2  
방산 납품 계획에 따른 일시적 실적 급감

방산 납품 계획에 따른 일시적 실적 급감 
지상방산 매출 1조 2,450억원으로 전년비 41%, 영업이익 217억원으로 전년비 
96.2% 급감하며 영업이익률은 1.7% 기록. 실적 급감에 대해 사측은 올해 주요 사업
의 납품 일정이 4분기에 집중됨에 따라 3분기 인도 물량이 적었던 것과 대전 사업장 
생산 중단에 따른 영향 때문이라고 설명. 단, 4분기에는 당초 계획에 따라 주요 사업의 
납품 물량이 집중되어 있어 실적이 개선될 것이고 대전 사업장은 내년 상반기 내에 정
상 가돌 하는 것이 목표라고 밝힘 
수주잔고 증가세 지속으로 성장 지속 가능할 것 
3분기 말 수주잔고 35조원으로 전년비 12.9% 증가. 이를 통한 지상방산 부문 성장 
지속 가능할 것으로 판단. 참고로 사측은 내년 매출 15% 수준 성장 전망 유지. 하지
만 금리 상승에 따른 글로벌 업종 평균 밸류에이션 하락에 따른 방산 부문 적용 멀티
플 하향 조정 그리고 상장 연결회사(한화오션, 한화시스템, 쎄트렉아이)와 상장 비연결
회사(한국항공우주)의 주가 하락 반영 영향으로 목표주가는 하향 조정 
8월 미 육군 차륜형 자주포 사업에서 시제 사업자로 선정. 향후 K9MH는 미 육군의 
작전 운용 개념에 맞춰 맞춤 조정을 거친 후에 특별한 결함 혹은 결격 사유가 없으면 
최종 계약 체결 예정. 동시에 미 육군의 궤도형 자주포 사업에도 참여할 계획. 뿐만 아
니라 스페인 K9, 크로아티아 천무 등으로 신시장 개척 역시 지속되고 있다는 점도 긍
정적 
 Analyst 이재광 
rheejaekwang@lssec.co.kr

2 of 8 October 26, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MACRO STRATEGY
Oil Price Dynamics Remain Key to the Outlook
Chief Investment Office, Macro Strategy Team
Inflation, interest rates and equity market dynamics have changed materially since mid-
August, as widening attacks on Middle East and Russian crude oil and refining capacity
worsened supply and price conditions. Indeed, with the oil market already strained, an
early-September attack on a major Saudi Arabian export pipeline sharply raised concerns

about a protracted and deepening oil crisis. While Middle East crude exports eventually
managed to significantly rebound, increased security risks and freight costs raised
physical crude prices.
Against this backdrop, the spot Brent price surged from $92/barrel in mid-August
back into a $115 to $135/barrel price range until recently. Reflecting unusual market
tightness, other North Sea oil grades were recently trading even higher, up to $160/
barrel, according to the International Energy Agency (IEA). Even as it cooled from $135

to $115/barrel between October 2 and October 20, spot Brent is still almost double
year-ago levels, and so are many diesel prices. The AAA average U.S. retail diesel price
recently hit a new record and was 74% higher year-over-year (YoY) as of October 20.
With insufficient regional supply, the New York Harbor wholesale diesel price is 130%
higher from a year ago, according to the Energy Information Administration (EIA).
Indeed, sharply higher oil prices and a growing diesel shortage have put the global diesel

market under extreme strain. The U.S., for example, has little distillate cushion and
limited spare refining capacity. The distillate inventory (diesel and fuel oil) has decreased
from 37 days of demand coverage in January to just 26 days by early October, according
to the EIA. This 30% drop brought the supply buffer to the bottom of its historical
range. With limited refining capacity and reliance on imports for about 10% of regional
consumption, East Coast distillate inventories are the lowest since 1990, for example,

and are expected to remain below their 5-year average into late 2027, according to EIA
data. More broadly, according to the IEA’s October Oil Market Report (OMR), “oil market
balances are forecast to remain in deficit through the end of the year, with constrained
middle distillate supplies likely to persist well into 2027.”
Thus, although wage growth/labor demand do not appear inflationary, the oil crisis
risks continuing to feed “core” inflation. According to the Federal Reserve’s Beige Book1

covering precisely the mid-August to early-October period of heightened oil-market
turmoil, “higher oil prices permeated all District reports,” with firms noting that fuel
surcharges were passing on increased costs throughout the supply chain. The 22% rise
in airfares over the past year alone contributed around 0.2 percentage point (pp) to the
2.6% 12-month increase in the “core” consumer price index (CPI) for September. Aside
from transportation costs effects, the spike in diesel prices also portends high food

prices with a few-month lag given planting/harvesting cost considerations.
By solidifying expectations for higher Fed interest rates to fight inflation, the surge in oil
prices has contributed to the spike in long-term Treasury yields this year. According to an
October 13, 2026, Empirical Research Partners report, the Iran-war oil crisis is estimated
to have contributed about 0.50 pp to the roughly 2.40 pp increase in the real U.S. long-
term Treasury rate from 2020 to early October (Exhibit 1A). According to the report, this

oil-price effect accounts for “much of the rise in real rates since February 27, 2026...”
On the positive side, while most other underpinnings for higher rates compared to the
pre-pandemic decade are structural (Exhibit 1A), the oil-price-related effect on interest
rates is reversible, implying that a resolution to the current oil crisis has the potential to
help lower both inflation and interest rates, relieving some of the current headwinds to
growth and asset prices.

Indeed, while much of the increase in interest rates since the pandemic reflects stronger,
more normal economic growth, and has thus been accompanied by surging corporate
1 Summary of Commentary on Current Economic Conditions by Federal Reserve District, Federal Reserve, October 2026.

3 of 8 October 26, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
earnings and risk-asset prices, worsening oil-market conditions since mid-summer and
associated spike in yields have been accompanied by a loss of momentum in U.S. equity
prices (Exhibit 1B). Given their higher sensitivity to energy price and interest rates shocks,
Small-caps lost relatively more momentum than Large-caps during this period.
The Large-cap rally also faded, though, as oil and interest rate headwinds widened stock-

level return dispersion, with roughly 40% of constituent stocks in bear market territory
(down 20% or more). Still, buoyed by the Technology sector as well as by expectations
of conflict resolution, the market-capitalization weighted S&P 500 Index has mainly
consolidated just below its peak. While the equal-weighted S&P 500 Index stumbled more,
it was just about 3% off its peak as of October 20.
While the economy remains resilient and profit expectations remain strong, persistent

oil market turmoil makes the macro backdrop more fragile. For example, the Beige Book
not only highlights concern about spreading fuel-related inflation, as noted above, but its
language also suggests that economic activity lost some momentum since its mid-July
to late-August report. Overall, it describes growth as slight to moderate, with generally
moderate manufacturing growth except for robust activity in the energy-heavy Dallas
District. Little growth is reported in other sectors overall, with most Districts seeing little

to no change in consumer spending, for example.
Employment was described as stable to little changed, with generally adequate labor supply
except for certain specialized trade workers. Five of twelve Districts note some reports of
businesses cutting staff through attrition, delaying hiring or anticipating less job creation
as a result of AI-driven productivity gains. Most Districts reported that business maintained
a positive outlook for coming months, though “expectations tend to be for slight growth,

weighted toward manufacturing and burdened by persistent uncertainty.”
The September employment and retail sales reports seem to validate the anecdotal Beige
Book evidence. Also, the 0.4% annualized manufacturing production growth over the July to
September period (and 1.1% YoY, according to the Federal Reserve Broad), while welcome,
is hardly an indication of booming conditions. Against a backdrop of rising electricity prices,
wage growth lagging inflation, and higher gasoline prices broadly offsetting this year’s tax

cut benefits, real consumer spending is unlikely to accelerate much absent stronger labor
demand and real wage growth. A resolution to the oil crisis that brings economic relief and
tempers interest rates is thus critical for continued expansion in 2027, in our view.

Investment Implications
Portfolio maintenance is a key
component of successful investing.
It’s time for investors to assess their
portfolios—or look under the hood.
MARKET VIEW
Six Questions Investors Should Be Asking Themselves Right Now
Joseph Quinlan, Managing Director and Head of Market Strategy
After four years of rising equity prices, investors have plenty to celebrate. Despite

inflation scares, geopolitical tensions, trade wars, rising interest rates and breathless
predictions around AI, the S&P 500 has returned some 131% (total returns) since the
bull market in equities commenced in October 2022. Corporate earnings have generally
exceeded expectations; AI has ignited a historic investment boom, and America’s
technology giants have powered the market to new heights.
We believe the bull will rumble on into 2027. We also believe that now is the time for

investors to take stock of their portfolios, since bull markets have a way of changing the
composition of portfolios. In addition, investors should not assume that the investment
strategies that worked well in the past four years will necessarily deliver comparable
results during the next four. Below are six questions investors should be asking
themselves right now:
Number One: How concentrated is my portfolio? Many investors believe they are
invested in the stock market when, in fact, they are invested in a handful of companies—

specifically AI-related names that have soared and dominated the index over the past
few years. Information technology now accounts for roughly 40% of the market cap of
the S&P 500, while the top 10 companies represent a share of 38.4%.
As the Financial Times recently noted, U.S. equity and credit markets have become so
increasingly concentrated in AI-related groups that “more than two-thirds of companies
in the Russell 1000 index of large U.S. groups are linked to AI, either directly or

indirectly or because the technology is becoming embedded in their business models.”2
Meanwhile, Big Technology hyperscalers and companies that are beneficiaries of the AI
spending boom make up about 16% of the U.S. high-grade bond market after a huge
borrowing binge, making the grouping the largest in the investment-grade bond market.2
The upshot: Investors that have been rewarded by concentration need to ask themselves
what percentage of their wealth is tied to the all-encompassing AI trade and consider

what would happen if mega-cap tech leadership falters.
Number Two: How diversified is my portfolio? While concentration asks whether
investors own too much of a few assets, diversification asks whether investors own enough
of different assets that behave differently when economic cycles or financial conditions
change. A portfolio containing hundreds of U.S. Equities via exchange-traded funds, mutual
funds and other assets may appear diversified. But if those companies are overwhelmingly

exposed to the same economic cycle, interest rate environment, or technology theme,
the diversification benefits are diluted. Case in point: While we remain constructive on
Emerging Market (EM) Equities, investors need to understand that a significant driver of
EM returns has come from AI-driven markets like Taiwan and South Korea.
Today, true diversification means considering exposure to hard assets (commodities, real
estate), EM mining and agricultural leaders, EM bonds and currencies, China bio-technology,

Small-cap international, and Private Equity and Hedge Funds for qualified investors.
Number Three: How tax-efficient is my portfolio? Bull markets create wealth—in
addition to tax liabilities. The good news is that after four years of rising equity returns,
investors are sitting on sizable, unrealized capital gains. The bad news: Market gains
can feel like a financial straitjacket, making it difficult to rebalance portfolios without
triggering significant tax liabilities.

Against this backdrop, now is the time for investors to review where the assets are
held—distinguishing between taxable and tax-advantaged accounts, and the attendant tax
ramifications. The appropriate mix depends on individual circumstances, including future
2 “Investors seek shelter from heightened exposure to AI”, The Financial Times, October 6, 2026.

### Page 5

5 of 8 October 26, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
withdrawal taxes. It is also time to consider tax-loss strategies, charitable donations of
securities and other tactics and strategies since in the end, the measure of investment
success isn’t simply how much money investors make. It’s how much they keep.
Number Four: Is it time to take profits?* There is a peculiarity to bull markets: The
more money investors make, the more the attendant tax liabilities. Tax liabilities, in turn,

can discourage selling, and the reluctance to sell can leave investors dangerously exposed
to yesterday’s leaders and compound concentration risks. That brings up an emotionally
difficult decision: When should investors sell their leaders or take some chips off the table?
The question is notably relevant today, given four years of substantial Equity gains.
Beyond tax considerations, investors need to understand that profit-taking can create
financial flexibility. Realized after-tax gains can replenish cash reserves, fund near-

term spending needs, reduce leverage or most importantly, provide capital to rebalance
portfolios in other areas of the market. In general, the goal of taking profits isn’t to get
out of the market—it is to ensure that after years of accumulated gains, these proceeds
are redeployed and redistributed across various asset classes that continue to support
and underpin long-term wealth accumulation.
Number Five: How well is my portfolio positioned for a new interest rate

regime? For much of the past quarter century, investors operated in a world of copious
liquidity, declining interest rates and relatively cheap capital. The era of capital abundance
favored stocks over bonds, but that era is over—we are shifting to a world of capital
scarcity, or intense competition for capital and the attendant hike in the cost of capital.
In this world, higher rates weigh the most on long-duration assets. Positioning for this
regime favors companies with strong balance sheets and cash flow over firms with heavy

debt or refinancing needs. High-quality bonds are more attractive in this environment;
sovereign debt risks are concentrated more overseas (i.e., France) than the U.S.
Number Six: How well is my portfolio positioned for the next shock? Shocks
happen. If the past four years have taught investors anything, it is that the unexpected
should be the expected. This decade alone has produced gut-wrenching market moves
related to a global pandemic, a ground war in Europe, the reconfiguration of global

supply chains, epic technological change and a war in the Middle East. Through it all, U.S.
Equities have trended higher but hardly in a linear fashion.
The next shocks could emerge in the global bond market, where mounting government
debt and higher borrowing costs have triggered mounting volatility. It could emerge from
disappointing returns on massive AI investments. Or it could come from a geopolitical
event, another energy shock or any other unforeseen event. Investors can’t predict the

next shock, but they can prepare for it. So now is the time for investors to stress test
their portfolios against a potential shock, examining how a steep downdraft in Equities
or spike in bond yields would affect their retirement plans, liquidity needs and other
financial goals and objectives.
Exhibit 2: Six Questions to Stress Test Your Portfolio.
The six questions What investors should examine Portfolio diagnostic
1. How concentrated am I? Mega-cap technology exposure and overlapping holdings % of portfolio in top 10 stocks

2. Am I truly diversified? Asset allocation, geographic mix and correlated exposures Current equity/bond mix versus target
3. How tax-efficient am I? Unrealized gains, asset location and tax-loss harvesting Embedded capital gains and estimated tax cost of rebalancing
4. Is it time to take profits? Portfolio drift, oversized winners and valuations Positions exceeding target allocation
5. Am I ready for higher interest rates? Duration, leverage, refinancing risks and cash flows Bond duration and exposure to highly leveraged companies

6. Am I prepared for the next shock? Liquidity, downside exposure and portfolio resilience Estimated loss in a 20% equity selloff

THE TICKER TAPE
What are rising credit spreads signaling about the broader market environment?
Christopher Hyzy, Chief Investment Officer
Rising French bond yields with a sharp rise in spreads above German bunds, mixed
with AI buildout concerns, are creating nervousness across the capital markets. This
nervousness centers around the ability to fund deficits, the potential for slower growth

and the future return on the announced capital investment. We expect these concerns
to continue to linger well into next year as growth worries remain in France, bond
yields stay sticky around current levels, and, in the case of AI, investors demand more
compensation for financing.
At this stage, we do not expect the rising spreads in targeted areas to be a precursor
for a material slowdown in economic growth overall. Rather, we believe this is a natural

signal that investors are moving up the quality stack as we move into the middle of
the AI buildout. The bottom line is that rising credit spreads in lower quality corners of
the market are natural along the lifecycle of financing. When free cash flow is called
into question, or when debt financing costs begin to approach the expected return on
investment, investors begin to reprice risk.
We continue to emphasize higher quality balance sheets in corporate and sovereign

credit as well as higher quality leaders in the AI buildout overall. The closer we get to
2027, investors will likely begin to transition from too much exposure to the “buildout”
and not enough exposure to “AI adopters.”

Index Definitions
Securities indexes assume reinvestment of all distributions and interest payments. Indexes are unmanaged and do not take into account fees or expenses. It is not possible to
invest directly in an index. Indexes are all based in U.S. dollars.
SS&P 500 Index is a stock market index tracking the stock performance of 500 leading companies listed on stock exchanges in the United States.

Consumer Price Index is a statistical measure that tracks the average change over time in the prices paid by urban consumers for a representative "market basket" of everyday goods and
services.
S&P 500 Market-cap Weighted Index is a float-adjusted market-capitalization-weighted index, meaning a company's influence and percentage weight in the index are determined by its
total market value (share price multiplied by the number of outstanding shares).

S&P 500 Equal-Weighted Index is a variation of the traditional S&P 500 where every single company in the index is given the exact same weight—0.2%—regardless of its size or market
capitalization.
S&P 500 Equal-Weighted Industrials Index imposes equal weights on the index constituents included in the S&P 500 that are classified in the GICS® industrials sector.
Russell 1000 Index is a stock market index that tracks the performance of the 1,000 largest publicly traded companies in the United States.

Bank of America, Merrill, their affiliates and advisors do not provide legal, tax or accounting advice. Clients should consult their legal and/or tax advisors before making any financial decisions.
This material does not take into account a client’s particular investment objectives, financial situations, or needs and is not intended as a recommendation, offer, or solicitation for the purchase
or sale of any security or investment strategy. Merrill offers a broad range of brokerage, investment advisory and other services. There are important differences between brokerage and

of America, Merrill or any affiliate to sell or provide, or a specific invitation for a consumer to apply for, any particular retail financial product or service that may be available.
The Chief Investment Office (“CIO”) provides thought leadership on wealth management, investment strategy and global markets; portfolio management solutions; due diligence; and solutions
oversight and data analytics. CIO viewpoints are developed for Bank of America Private Bank, a division of Bank of America, N.A., (“Bank of America”) and Merrill Lynch, Pierce, Fenner & Smith

The Global Wealth & Investment Management Investment Strategy Committee (“GWIM ISC”) is responsible for developing and coordinating recommendations for short-term and long-term
investment strategy and market views encompassing markets, economic indicators, asset classes and other market-related projections affecting GWIM.
BofA Global Research is research produced by BofA Securities, Inc. (“BofAS”) and/or one or more of its affiliates. BofAS is a registered broker-dealer, Member SIPC and wholly owned subsidiary

of Bank of America Corporation.
All recommendations must be considered in the context of an individual investor’s goals, time horizon, liquidity needs and risk tolerance. Not all recommendations will be in the best interest
of all investors.
Asset allocation, diversification and rebalancing do not ensure a profit or protect against loss in declining markets.
Investments have varying degrees of risk. Some of the risks involved with equity securities include the possibility that the value of the stocks may fluctuate in response to events specific to

the companies or markets, as well as economic, political or social events in the U.S. or abroad. Small cap and mid cap companies pose special risks, including possible illiquidity and greater price
volatility than funds consisting of larger, more established companies. Investing in fixed-income securities may involve certain risks, including the credit quality of individual issuers, possible
prepayments, market or economic developments and yields and share price fluctuations due to changes in interest rates. When interest rates go up, bond prices typically drop, and vice versa.

Investments in high-yield bonds (sometimes referred to as “junk bonds”) offer the potential for high current income and attractive total return, but involves certain risks. Changes in economic
conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect a junk bond issuer’s ability to make principal and interest payments. Income from investing in municipal bonds is generally exempt
from Federal and state taxes for residents of the issuing state. While the interest income is tax-exempt, any capital gains distributed are taxable to the investor. Income for some investors

may be subject to the Federal Alternative Minimum Tax (AMT). Treasury bills are less volatile than longer-term fixed income securities and are guaranteed as to timely payment of principal and
interest by the U.S. government. Bonds are subject to interest rate, inflation and credit risks. Investments in foreign securities (including ADRs) involve special risks, including foreign currency
risk and the possibility of substantial volatility due to adverse political, economic or other developments. These risks are magnified for investments made in emerging markets. Investments in

a certain industry or sector may pose additional risk due to lack of diversification and sector concentration. There are special risks associated with an investment in commodities, such as gold,
including market price fluctuations, regulatory changes, interest rate changes, credit risk, economic changes and the impact of adverse political or financial factors.
Alternative investments are speculative and involve a high degree of risk.
Alternative investments are intended for qualified investors only. Alternative Investments such as derivatives, hedge funds, private credit, private equity funds, and funds of funds can result

in higher return potential but also higher loss potential. Changes in economic conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect your investments. Before you invest in alternative
investments, you should consider your overall financial situation, how much money you have to invest, your need for liquidity, and your tolerance for risk.
Nonfinancial assets, such as closely held businesses, real estate, fine art, oil, gas and mineral properties, and timber, farm and ranch land, are complex in nature and involve risks including total

### Page 1

< Real GDP growth forecast at 0.9% in FY 2026, 
1.3% in FY 2027, and 1.1% in FY 2028 > 
1. Real GDP growth in the July–September quarter of 2026 was −0.1% on an annualized basis, 
marking the first contraction in four quarters, mainly due to a decline in private consumption. 
2. Real GDP growth in the October–December quarter of 2026 and the January–March quarter of 
2027 is expected to remain in the low- to mid-0% range. This is due in part to weak private 
consumption, driven mainly by declining real purchasing power as inflation accelerates. 
Consequently, Japan’s economy is likely to remain sluggish in the second half of FY 2026. 
3. In FY 2027, lower inflation following the consumption tax rate cut on food and beverages is 
expected to improve households’ real purchasing power and lead to a recovery in private 
consumption. Meanwhile, growth in private non-residential investment is expected to accelerate as 
lower raw material prices improve corporate profits. 
4. Real GDP growth is forecast at 0.9% in FY 2026, 1.3% in FY 2027, and 1.1% in FY 2028. The 
consumption tax rate cut is estimated to lower real GDP by 0.1% in FY 2026 and raise it by 0.3% 
in FY 2027. 
5. Consumer price inflation (all items excluding fresh food) is expected to accelerate to the 3% range 
toward the end of FY 2026 as rising import prices feed through to a broad range of items, 
including food and daily necessities. However, it is projected to slow to the 1% range in FY 2027 
following the consumption tax rate cut on food and beverages. Overall, consumer price inflation 
(all items excluding fresh food) is forecast at 2.4% in FY 2026, 1.5% in FY 2027, and 2.0% in FY 
2028.

1 AI-related goods comprise the 103 product categories classified as “AI-Enabling-Products” by the WTO. AI-related goods accounted for 
17.1% of total exports in 2025.

### Page 3

AI-related goods have recorded pronounced
increases in both prices and volumes, likely
reflecting tighter supply and demand conditions
for semiconductors and electronic components as
global AI investment expands. Export prices of
AI-related goods rose sharply by 24.3% year-on-
year in the July–September quarter of 2026.
Together with the increase in export volumes,
this resulted in strong 38.0% year-on-year
growth in export value. Expanding AI-related

exports are substantially boosting total export value on both a volume and price basis.
These increases in export prices, led by AI-related goods, are also helping to mitigate the deterioration in the
terms of trade associated with heightened tensions in the Middle East. While higher prices for crude oil and
other commodities worsen the terms of trade through rising import prices, export prices have also increased
substantially. As a result, the deterioration in the terms of trade index—calculated by dividing export prices

by import prices—has been relatively limited.
Looking at past episodes of surging commodity prices, during the global commodity price surge around
2005–2008, import prices for crude oil and other commodities rose sharply while export price increases
failed to keep pace, leading to a deterioration in the terms of trade. From 2020 onward, the terms of trade
also deteriorated significantly due to rising commodity prices during the post-COVID-19 global economic

recovery, the surge in commodity prices following Russia’s invasion of Ukraine in 2022, and yen
depreciation. In both episodes, the cumulative deterioration in the terms of trade was at least 30%. By
comparison, the deterioration over the two quarters from the January–March quarter to the July–September
quarter of 2026 was 6.6%. A major difference from past episodes is that higher export prices, led by AI-
related goods, have partially offset the impact

of rising import prices caused by surging
crude oil prices.
However, import prices are still rising faster
than export prices, and the terms of trade
continue to deteriorate. If crude oil prices
remain elevated, income outflows abroad
could weigh on the domestic economy.
Meanwhile, if export prices continue to rise
as AI-related demand expands, this will help
mitigate further deterioration in the terms of
trade.

### Page 4

The wage hike rate in the 2027 Spring Labor Offensive (Shunto) is expected to slightly exceed
the previous year’s level
Real wages, calculated by deflating nominal wages using consumer prices (all items excluding imputed rent),
have maintained positive growth for nine consecutive months since January 2026. Nominal wage (total cash
earnings) growth accelerated from the low-2% range in 2025 to the 3% range after the start of 2026. In
addition, a substantial slowdown in inflation—partly due to government measures to ease the burden of

energy costs—has contributed to the increase in real wages.
Although real wages have recently begun to
recover, they had remained on a downward trend
for approximately four years from 2022, as
nominal wage growth failed to keep pace with
inflation. Compared with the pre-COVID-19
period in 2019, nominal wages in the July–
September quarter of 2026 were 12.6% higher,
whereas consumer prices (all items excluding
imputed rent) had risen by an even larger 16.8%

over the same period. Thus, despite the recovery
since the start of 2026, real wages remain 3.7%
below pre-COVID-19 levels, meaning nominal wage increases have not yet fully offset the erosion in
purchasing power caused by higher prices.
The environment surrounding the 2027 Spring Labor Offensive shows that labor market conditions remain
tight, with the active job openings-to-applicants ratio above 1 and the unemployment rate in the mid-2%
range. Corporate profits continue to reach new record highs despite the impact of heightened tensions in the

Middle East. Consumer price inflation had remained in the 1% range since the start of 2026 due to
government energy relief measures, but it rose to the 2% range in September for the first time in nine months
and is certain to accelerate.
In its basic framework for the 2027 Spring Labor Offensive announced on October 22, the Japanese Trade
Union Confederation (Rengo) set a wage hike guideline of “around 6%, with a minimum of 5% (including
the equivalent of regular pay increases),” raising its demands from the previous year’s “at least 5%.” It also

set a guideline of “¥20,000 and at least 6%” for unions at small and midsize companies, including an
additional amount to narrow wage disparities. In recent years, employers’ offers in the Spring Labor
Offensive have generally been slightly less than 1 percentage point below union demands. Since 2023, labor
unions’ wage demands have risen sharply due to high prices and labor shortages, and actual wage hike rates
have risen accordingly, keeping the gap between demands and offers relatively stable. Given this pattern, if

Rengo’s affiliated unions raise their wage demands in line with its policy, employers are also likely to raise
their offers, pushing up the overall wage hike rate.

### Page 5

Against this backdrop, the current outlook
forecasts a wage hike rate of 5.10% for the 2027
Spring Labor Offensive, remaining high in the
5% range for the fourth consecutive year and
rising slightly from 5.01% in the previous year
(on the Ministry of Health, Labour and Welfare
basis, the forecast is 5.30% for 2027, compared
with the actual result of 5.18% in 2026). The
strong possibility that consumer price inflation
will accelerate from its current level of around

2% to the 3% range around the beginning of 2027—when wage negotiations get into full swing—should also
support wage increases.
Real wages have continued to grow by around 2% year-on-year, but growth is likely to slow gradually as
consumer price inflation accelerates, temporarily turning negative toward the end of FY 2026. However,
growth is expected to turn positive after the start of FY 2027 as the consumption tax rate cut on food and
beverages slows consumer price inflation. In FY 2028, once the effect of the consumption tax rate cut runs

its course, consumer price inflation will rise to around 2%. However, continued large wage hikes will sustain
nominal wage growth in the 3% range, keeping real wage growth at around 1%.
2. Real GDP growth forecast at 0.9% in FY 2026, 1.3% in FY 2027, and 1.1% in
FY 2028
Low growth expected to continue in the second half of FY 2026
The decline in private consumption—the main cause of negative growth in the July–September quarter of
2026—largely reflected the temporary effects of adverse weather. Even after these effects dissipate, however,

accelerating inflation is likely to hinder a recovery in consumption. Consumer price inflation returned to the
2% range in September 2026 for the first time in nine months, but is likely to rise to the 3% range toward the
end of FY 2026 as the pass-through of higher raw material prices to durable goods and daily necessities
becomes more widespread. Private consumption is therefore expected to remain weak in the second half of
FY 2026 as higher inflation erodes real purchasing power.

At present, growth is expected to turn positive in the October–December quarter of 2026, but with private
consumption struggling to grow, it is projected to reach only 0.6% on an annualized basis. In the January–
March quarter of 2027, private consumption is likely to decline quarter-on-quarter as inflation rises to the 3%
range and consumers hold off on purchases ahead of the consumption tax rate cut on food and beverages.
Real GDP growth is therefore likely to remain close to zero, at 0.2% on an annualized basis. Overall, Japan’s

economy is expected to remain sluggish in the second half of FY 2026, mainly because high prices depress
private consumption.

### Page 6

Private non-residential investment continues to recover on an underlying basis, supported by high corporate
profits, but the pace of recovery is likely to remain modest for the time being due to high construction
material prices and delays in construction progress caused by labor shortages. Although global AI-related
demand will support exports, overall growth is expected to remain modest given the persistent drag from the
situation in the Middle East and subdued shipments to China remaining amid weakness in its domestic

demand. As a result, exports are unlikely to drive economic growth.
In FY 2027, lower inflation following the consumption tax rate cut is expected to improve households’ real
purchasing power and lead to a recovery in private consumption. Meanwhile, growth in private non-
residential investment is expected to accelerate as lower raw material prices improve corporate profits.
Growth in the April–June quarter of 2027 is likely to be strong at 2.8% on an annualized basis, mainly driven

by a surge in private consumption as improved real purchasing power reinforces the rebound from pre-tax-
cut spending restraint. Thereafter, growth is expected to continue in the low-1% range on an annualized
basis—above the potential growth rate—led by domestic private demand such as private consumption and
non-residential investment. In FY 2028, although the effect of the consumption tax rate cut will fade, the
continued recovery in private consumption and ongoing increases in capital investment are likely to sustain

growth of around 1% on an annualized basis.
Real GDP growth is forecast at 0.9% in FY
2026, 1.3% in FY 2027, and 1.1% in FY 2028.
The institute estimates that consumers holding
off on purchases ahead of the consumption tax
rate cut on food and beverages will lower real
GDP in FY 2026 by 0.1%, while the subsequent
rebound and improved real purchasing power
from lower inflation will raise real GDP in FY
2027 by 0.3%.
Households’ real disposable income remains below its pre-COVID-19 level

Sluggish household consumption since the start of FY 2026 partly reflects the reclassification of spending as
government consumption following the introduction of free high school tuition and elementary school meals,
as well as adverse weather, including heavy and prolonged rain. Over a longer horizon, however, weak
growth in real disposable income is the primary cause. Households’ real disposable income (seasonally
adjusted) has recently recovered but remains slightly below its pre-COVID-19 level (the 2019 average).
