### Page 8

Private consumption is forecast to grow by 0.0% in FY 2026, 1.4% in FY 2027, and 0.9% in FY 2028 year-
on-year.
Price outlook
The rate of increase in consumer prices (all items excluding fresh food, hereafter core CPI) fell to 1.9% year-
on-year in January 2026, dropping below 2% for the first time in three years and ten months (since March
2022). It subsequently remained in the 1% range before rising to 2.1% year-on-year in September, returning
to the 2% range for the first time in nine months.

The rate of increase in food prices (excluding fresh food) continued to slow after peaking at 8.3% year-on-
year in July 2025, reaching 2.7% in August 2026 before accelerating for the first time in 14 months to 2.9%
in September. According to Teikoku Databank’s “Survey of Price Trends at 195 Major Food Companies”
(released October 30), the number of food and beverage items subject to price increases in 2026 (January–
December) is 20,327, slightly below the 20,580 items recorded in the same period of the previous year. Amid

the surge in raw material prices associated with the deteriorating situation in the Middle East, the number of
items slated for price increases over the next three months has risen year-on-year since June 2026, although
the pace has slowed somewhat. The rate of increase in consumer food prices (excluding fresh food) is
expected to accelerate, though it should remain more moderate than in the summer of 2025, partly because
rice prices are expected to continue declining for the time being.

Meanwhile, energy prices have been restrained by the government’s relief measures, but support for
electricity and gas bills temporarily ended with September 2026 usage (reflected in October consumer
prices). Although support will resume for usage starting in December 2026, electricity and gas bills this
winter are expected to be substantially higher than in the previous year. This is because subsidy amounts will
be slightly lower than last year, and the impact of surging crude oil prices since the spring of 2026 will be

reflected in fuel cost adjustment rates with a lag. Additionally, to curb surging gasoline prices, the
government used subsidies to hold the nationwide average retail price of regular gasoline at approximately
¥170 per liter from March through November 2026, but it has announced plans to gradually reduce subsidies
from December onward and raise the benchmark price by ¥5 each month. Consequently, energy price
inflation is expected to accelerate sharply from −0.6% year-on-year in September 2026, reaching double

digits year-on-year after the start of 2027 and contributing more than 1 percentage point to core CPI
inflation.
This outlook assumes that support for electricity and gas bills will continue from the summer of 2027
onward, with subsidies gradually reduced. For gasoline subsidies, the benchmark price is assumed to be
raised in stages to ¥185 per liter in February 2027 and then held at that level.
Core CPI inflation is expected to continue trending upward during FY 2026 and reach the 3% range toward

the end of FY 2026 as rising import prices, led by crude oil, feed through to a broad range of items, including
energy, food, durable goods, and daily necessities. A mechanical estimate of the impact of the consumption
tax rate cut for food and beverages from April 2027 onward indicates that core CPI inflation will be reduced

Document 367: Meta将Muse智能体接入Instagram与沃尔玛

Source PDF: all report pdf/Meta将Muse智能体接入Instagram与沃尔玛.pdf

### Page 1

沃尔玛与 Meta 达成合作，⽤⼾可通过 AI 助⼿ Muse 搜索⿊五促销商品、⽐较价格并完成下
单，⽆需浏览沃尔玛⽹站。合作由沃尔玛 Agent Gateway 系统⽀持，该系统将向更多 AI 平
台开放。与此同时，沃尔玛持续升级⾃有助⼿ Sparky ，将 AI 功能延伸⾄线上购物和线下⻔
店导航。
摘要：
沃尔玛正尝试让消费者通过 AI 助⼿直接完成购物。当地时间周⼆，沃尔玛宣布与 Meta 达成合
作，⽤⼾可以通过 Meta 旗下 AI 智能体 Muse 购买沃尔玛的⿊⾊星期五促销商品，⽆需⾃⾏浏览沃
尔玛⽹站，即可完成商品搜索、⽐价和下单。
具体⽽⾔，⽤⼾打开 Muse 应⽤并登录沃尔玛账号后，只需描述所需商品， Muse 便会向沃尔玛
发送请求，由沃尔玛返回相关商品信息，帮助⽤⼾⽐较选项、寻找优惠并⽣成购物⻋。
沃尔玛表⽰，合作体验内置了隐私保护机制。Muse ⽆法访问⽤⼾的沃尔玛购物历史及其他个⼈
信息；消费者可以设置消费限额、要求购买前审批，并查看 AI 代理提交的订单，也可以随时删

除代理档案。
此次合作意味着沃尔玛开始通过外部 AI 助⼿拓展购物⼊⼝。⽤⼾不再必须进⼊零售商⾃有⽹站搜
索商品，⽽是可以将购物需求交给 AI 代理处理。
开放 Agent Gateway ，沃尔玛争夺 AI 购物新⼊⼝
此次合作由沃尔玛今年 10 ⽉上线的代理⽹关系统（ Agent Gateway ）提供⽀持。该系统允许外
部 AI 助⼿直接连接沃尔玛线上商店，获取商品信息并协助消费者购物。
沃尔玛计划向更多开发者和 AI 平台开放 Agent Gateway 。这意味着未来接⼊沃尔玛的 AI 助⼿可能
不⽌ Muse ，更多外部平台也有机会通过这⼀系统提供购物服务。 Instacart 近期也与 Meta 旗下
Muse 达成了合作。
沃尔玛与亚⻢逊在这⼀领域采取了不同策略。亚⻢逊已采取措施阻⽌第三⽅ AI 代理代替消费者完
成购买，⽽沃尔玛选择主动开放零售接⼝，允许外部 AI 助⼿参与商品搜索和交易流程。
两种路线反映出零售商对 AI 时代电商⼊⼝的不同判断：亚⻢逊倾向于维护⾃⾝平台的交易闭环，

沃尔玛则希望借助外部 AI 助⼿扩⼤商品触达范围，将其转化为新的购物渠道。
升级⾃有助⼿ Sparky ， AI 延伸⾄线下购物
在与外部 AI 平台合作的同时，沃尔玛也在升级⾃有购物助⼿ Sparky ，将 AI 应⽤延伸⾄实体⻔
店。
近期推出的新功能包括将⼿写购物清单转化为购物⻋、通过照⽚和语⾳指令搜索商品，以及帮
助消费者在⻔店内定位商品。⽤⼾搜索商品后，可以通过⻔店地图查看其所在货架； “ 闪现价签 ”
（ Flash Price Tag ）功能还可以让⽬标商品的货架价签亮起，⽅便消费者寻找。
从 Muse 接⼊外部购物场景，到 Sparky 覆盖线上搜索和线下寻货，沃尔玛正在将 AI 嵌⼊消费者购
物的不同环节。随着 Agent Gateway 向更多平台开放，外部 AI 助⼿能否为沃尔玛带来更多实际订
单，以及 AI 代理是否会逐渐成为电商购物的重要⼊⼝，将成为后续值得关注的⽅向。

Oil prices moved lower this morning despite further attacks
around the Strait of Hormuz, with recovering Gulf exports and a
sharp loosening in the Dubai crude curve signalling a more
comfortable supply picture

Ewa Manthey and Warren Patterson
Oil slips as improving supply outweighs disruption risks
Energy – Oil slips as improving supply
outweighs disruption risks
Oil prices opened more than 1% lower this morning, with Brent
hovering around $90/bbl and WTI near $80/bbl, as recovering
supply continued to outweigh escalating security risks around the
Strait of Hormuz. Iran has claimed that at least six tankers have
been targeted since Tuesday and threatened tighter control over
the waterway, but exports from the Persian Gulf continue to recover.
The improvement in near-term supply is also showing up in the
Dubai crude curve. The spread between November and January
contracts has moved to around $1.20/bbl in contango, compared

The sharp reversal points to a considerably more comfortable spot
market as regional ﬂows recover.
In the US, API ﬁgures showed crude inventories increasing by just
126k barrels last week, well below expectations for a 2.1m barrel
build. Gasoline stocks were broadly unchanged, while distillate
inventories rose by 1.6m barrels. Attention now turns to the EIA
inventory report due later today.

Meanwhile, OPEC+ heads into this weekend’s meeting with some
disagreement over production capacity. Iraq has reportedly rejected
an assessment putting its capacity at around 5m b/d, arguing that
it can produce as much as 5.2m b/d. The disagreement could
complicate discussions over future production baselines and supply
policy.
Metals – Aluminium squeeze eases after
LME intervention; copper extends sell-off
The historic squeeze in the London aluminium market is starting to

unwind after the LME introduced measures aimed at limiting the
inﬂuence of large positions and easing pressure on nearby
contracts. The cash-to-three-month spread fell to $67.53/t on
Tuesday from more than $400/t on Monday – a record high.
The exchange lowered the threshold at which its lending rules apply
to large positions from 50% to 30% of available stocks, while
introducing tougher requirements for positions held for longer
periods. The changes take eﬀect on Wednesday and are intended

to reduce the risk that concentrated positions exacerbate tightness
in nearby spreads.
The measures come after aluminium spreads surged to levels not
seen since the 1980s as historically low LME inventories collided
with large long positions. The squeeze has been concentrated at
the front of the curve rather than reﬂecting a broadly bullish outlook
for aluminium fundamentals.
The LME is also moving to increase the amount of metal that can
enter its warehouse system. The exchange is reportedly close to

in China, where premiums and spreads remain elevated as buyers
compete for prompt metal.

tariﬀs is also starting to be oﬀered back to Chinese buyers,
reversing part of the ﬂows that helped tighten supply elsewhere
earlier this year.

Document 393: The Global Economy’s Resilience to Shocks_ Four Observations and Three Intertwined Questions (2026-11-16)

Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-11-16
Pages: 2
Status: ok

### Page 1

The bank
for a changing

3
economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoWeek 26.42 / 16 November 2026EDITORIAL
THE GLOBAL ECONOMY’S RESILIENCE TO SHOCKS: FOUR OBSERVATIONS AND THREE INTERTWINED QUESTIONS
A review of the latest economic indicators reveals four key observations. First, the global economy continues to show re-
silience in the face of the energy shock, which has recently been compounded by the impact of rising long-term interest
rates. Second, this resilience still relies heavily on the global investment cycle, but not exclusively: household consump-

tion also provides support. Albeit on a more or less strong footing depending on the country, its contribution to growth
is non-negligible. Third, the rise in inflation remains primarily energy-driven, with the United States standing out unfa-
vorably from the Eurozone due to a more entrenched and persistent problem. Fourth, the rise in long-term government
yields is placing a significant, well-identified, yet manageable strain on public finances. Three intertwined questions arise

regarding the rise in long-term interest rates: How high can they go? Will growth continue to hold up against all odds?
How will central banks react?
FIRST OBSERVATION: THE GLOBAL ECONOMY CONTINUES TO WEATHER THE
ENERGY SHOCK WITHOUT BREAKING
The surge in energy prices following the outbreak of the Middle East
conflict in late February has had a smaller impact on economic acti -
vity than the 2022 energy shock caused by the invasion of Ukraine 1.

Although the price of a barrel of Brent crude once again (and largely so)
crossed the USD 100 threshold in October while European natural gas
prices (TTF) remain under pressure as winter approaches, the macroe-
conomic shockwave has remained much more contained than four
years ago. According to business climate surveys in the Eurozone and
the United States, the manufacturing sector has weathered the shock
remarkably well, while the impact was more visible in the services

sector and on consumer confidence before an improvement set in.
In October, the overall picture remains positive: while there are signs of
erosion, they remain contained. The PMI/ISM indices remain above the
50-point expansion threshold — clearly so in the US, and to a slightly
lesser extent in Europe. Emerging economies have also shown resi -
lience, thereby fueling global growth. Non-resident portfolio invest -
ment flows have certainly been highly volatile since the outbreak of the

conflict in Iran, but financing conditions have held up despite pressures
on energy prices and interest rates in advanced economies, and the
aggregate manufacturing PMI index for the fifteen major emerging eco-
nomies has nearly returned to its February 2026 level since September.
SECOND OBSERVATION: THE SUPPORTIVE ROLE OF HOUSEHOLD CONSUMP-
TION, IN THE SHADOW OF THE INVESTMENT CYCLE
In the current business cycle, there is much more talk of corporate in-

vestment than of household consumption. The former is indeed riding
high, driven by several mutually reinforcing trends: the AI boom, in -
creased military spending, decarbonization and electrification efforts,
and Germany’s massive infrastructure plan. As a result, this invest -
ment engine appears immune — or at least less sensitive, at the aggre-
gate level — to the energy shock than household consumption. Inco -
me and financing constraints appear to be more severe for household

consumption. This is evident in household confidence, which certainly
does not reflect the same level of optimism as business climate indica-
tors. And yet, while they show little confidence, they are quite spend -
thrift, particularly in the United States. Across the Atlantic, the current
investment cycle is certainly a major driver of growth, closely trailing
that of household consumption. However, until the second quarter of
2026, household consumption retained its role as the primary driver, as

having the largest contribution to growth (see chart). And while it was
dethroned in the third quarter according to currently available data, its
support remains very much present and substantial.
Household consumption in the Eurozone certainly does not show the
same momentum: on a quarterly average, from the first quarter of
2023 through the second quarter of 2026, it grew nearly half as fast
as in the United States (0.34% vs. 0.66%). Nevertheless, it remains the

dominant driver of growth in the Eurozone (with a contribution amoun-
ting to 0.2pp, or 71%, compared with half a percentage point for total
investment), without the same wealth effect as in the United States
and without a decline in the savings rate, which leaves it with greater
potential for improvement.
THIRD OBSERVATION: THE RISE IN INFLATION REMAINS MOSTLY ENER-
GY-DRIVEN BUT APPEARS MORE ENTRENCHED ACROSS THE ATLANTIC
On the inflation front, the rise continues to be primarily energy-driven,

even though it appears more entrenched in the United States, and ini-
tial signs of pass-through — notably via producer prices — will be
closely monitored, while overall upward pressures remain strong 2.
In the Eurozone, where harmonised inflation reached 4.0% year-on-
year in October 2026, energy accounts for nearly half of the rise in
prices. Core inflation remains contained (at 2.6% y/y), as do all alter -
native indicators, including inflation expectations. The situation in the

United States, however, calls for increased vigilance. While headline
inflation (3.7% y/y in September according to the CPI) is lower than the

Eurozone October print, the underlying problem is more profound: core
inflation is picking up again, and nearly 60% of the components of the
US consumer price basket are rising by more than 3% (compared with
43% in the Eurozone).
FOURTH OBSERVATION: THE RISE IN LONG-TERM RATES ADDS A NEW, SIGNI-
FICANT BUT MANAGEABLE CONSTRAINT ON PUBLIC FINANCES
The return of 10-year sovereign yields to levels not seen in 25 years —

nearing 5% in the United States, in the United Kingdom, and in France
— brings back to the forefront a constraint that had previously faded:
debt service, while the state of public finances is generally worse today
than it was a quarter-century ago, with budgets under heavy strain
and facing difficult trade-offs3. Among the trends to watch, the spread
between the implicit interest rate on debt and nominal GDP growth
— which has long been favorable (i.e. negative) — is set to reverse

by the end of the decade in most European economies. It would re -
main negative but narrow significantly in Spain and the United States,
with Japan standing out as the exception, still enjoying a significantly
favorable spread. Another trend to watch is the magnitude of the in -
crease in the interest burden by 2032, which is forecasted to double,
for example, in France, Germany, and Japan, and reach 5% of GDP in
the United States.
Nevertheless, this additional constraint remains manageable in the

short and medium term thanks to two factors:
i/ the protective inertia of the average debt maturity when it is high, as
is the case in the Eurozone, Japan, and the United Kingdom;
ii/ the onset of gradual fiscal consolidation in most European countries
(except in Germany, which is implementing an investment stimulus
program).
The projected trajectories are, however, demanding and subject to po-
litical uncertainties. But at least there is a stated commitment to redu-

cing fiscal imbalances. This is not the case in the United States, where,
in addition, the relatively short average maturity of the debt exposes
the country to a faster pass-through of rate hikes to debt service.
THREE INTERTWINED QUESTIONS SURROUNDING THE RISE IN LONG-TERM
RATES
The rise in long-term sovereign rates has multiple causes, some of
which are positive (resilient growth, the AI boom, risk appetite). Fur -
thermore, for now, it remains broadly warranted and orderly. And as

long as this rise occurs alongside a rally in equity markets, overall
financial conditions are not particularly tightened as a result.
Since the rise in long-term rates is driven by multiple factors, we
should expect them to remain, at a minimum, at a high level or even to
rise further, even if some of the factors that have recently pushed them
higher begin to subside. According to our forecasts, the bulk of the rise
is likely behind us, as yields already factor in the resilience of growth

and residual monetary tightening. Long-term rates could then begin to
decline as fiscal consolidation gains credibility.
The recent turbulence in the tech sector has, however, shown that
what is gained on one front (reduced pressure on long-term rates as
safe-haven bonds regain favor) immediately creates another problem
(increased downside risk to economic activity from a reversal of the
AI boom). What matters for growth is the overall trend in monetary

and financial conditions, including access to bank credit. Recent data
point to first signs of tighter conditions, but from a favorable starting
point, and this tightening is helpful to dampen demand and curb asso-
ciated inflation. As long as it remains contained — which is the most
likely scenario at present — it should only moderately dampen growth
momentum without jeopardizing it. In France, for example, we will
continue to monitor this interplay between tailwinds and headwinds

through the cross-dynamics of business start-ups and bankruptcies4.
Nonetheless, the bulk of these questions presents a new conundrum
for central banks: to what extent should they accommodate the rise
in long-term rates, and to what extent does this rise interfere with
the conduct of monetary policy? Positively, by improving transmission?
But then, to what extent can they rely on this rise to do part of the
job without fueling the rise excessively (by tightening too much or too

little)? Or negatively, by leading to an excessive tightening of financial
conditions and access to credit? All of this argues in favor of the very
measured approach adopted so far by central banks, particularly the
Fed and the ECB. Further hikes in policy rates are to be expected, but
rather a few ones than a lot, in order to fine-tune and support the cur-
rent growth cycle for as long as possible.
Hélène Baudchon
helene.baudchon@bnpparibas.com

### Page 1

Deutsche Bank 
Research Institute

Why hasn’t there been a major 
financial crisis in recent years?

1 
Authors 
Henry Allen 
Macro Strategist

Peter Sidorov, CFA 
Senior Economist

Camilla Siazon 
Research Analyst

November 3, 2026 
— The economist Hyman Minsky warned, in essence, that “stability is destabilising”. He 
argued that a lengthy period of financial stability could be problematic, as it induced 
risk-taking and complacency amongst investors, laying the foundation for the next 
crisis. For example, lenders relax their terms and borrowers are more willing to extend 
themselves, as nobody wants to miss out. This dynamic makes economies inherently 
prone to financial panics and boom-bust cycles. 
— But since the 2008 crisis, and the subsequent sovereign crisis in the Eurozone, we 
have yet to see another financial crisis happen on a scale that had a lasting market 
and macroeconomic impact. There have been clear moments of concern, as in early 
2023 with the regional bank turmoil, but nothing that has caused a lasting impact. So 
this led us to ask why. 
— In this paper, we examine why the post-GFC era has seen fewer financial crises. 
Explanations include the reforms made after 2008 to avoid a repeat, the lower 
proportion of financial assets held by the banking system, as well as the speedier 
reaction of policymakers when wobbles have occurred. We then consider a few 
candidates that could cause the next crisis, including AI vulnerabilities, private credit, 
and stretched fiscal positions. 
— Ultimately, financial crises haven’t been abolished, but the reforms of recent years 
have made it harder for shocks to propagate through the traditional banking system. 
Moreover, policymakers have become far more inclined to intervene when initial signs 
of stress have emerged.

### Page 2

3 November 2026
Thematic Research
Page 2 Deutsche Bank AG
Section 1: What is a financial crisis, and what causes them?
There isn’t a single technical definition of a “financial crisis”, and they come in many
different types. Commonly, we think of banking crises like 2008, but they can also
encompass currency/balance of payments crises, debt crises and other forms too.
Given currency and sovereign crises have been more common in emerging markets
(the UK's ERM crisis in 1992 and the euro crisis being the two most notable DM

occurrences), historical analysis in developed economies has tended to focus
heavily on banking crises. Non-bank funding or market-liquidity crises are often not
categorised separately in the literature, but they have become more common in
recent years. This includes the "dash-for-cash" stress at the onset of the Covid
pandemic in March 2020 and the 2022 LDI crisis in the UK.
But for all the different types of financial crisis, there are common themes between

them. Often, they are accompanied by asset bubbles that burst in a non-linear
fashion. We generally see a severe tightening in credit conditions, which in turn
flows through into a broader economic contraction. Frequently, there are
contagion-like dynamics that can be difficult to stop. And in the modern world, they
usually require policymakers to step in, stabilising the situation with interventions.
At risk of oversimplification, we note four factors that determine the emergence and

the severity of a financial crisis. The first three are well-established in the financial
literature, sometimes pooled together into a build-up and a crisis phase
(Brunnermeier & Oehmke 2013), while the fourth has become increasingly
important over the past 15-20 years:
1.  Underlying imbalances. Bubbles and financial imbalances build during
the run-up phase, leaving accumulated risks that materialise when the
crisis erupts. This is the build-up typically described by the early phases of

Kindleberger's model of financial crises.
2.  Trigger. This is the shock that turns an existing vulnerability into clear
financial stress. These are often very difficult to predict and we mostly leave
these outside the focus of this report.
3.  Amplification and financial system fragility. Various factors determine
whether a shock propagates through the financial system, and the
intensity of that shock. These include leverage, liquidity and maturity

mismatches, interconnectedness, and the concentration of exposures.
Other channels in the financial system can then amplify the shock,
including bank runs, fire sales and margin calls, and negative net worth
effects, sovereign–bank loops etc.
4.  Intervention/policy response. This has been increasingly important in
recent decades as the size and scope of policy response to crises has
increased. The outright size can vary, and intervention can occur across

several dimensions, ranging from direct intervention in financial markets,
to counter-cyclical macroeconomic policy that mitigates the impact on the
real economy.
Clearly, these factors are not independent and the interaction between them is what
determines the severity of a financial crisis.

### Page 3

3 November 2026
Thematic Research
Deutsche Bank AG Page 3
One important aspect is the joint role that underlying imbalances and amplification
play. While theoretical models such as Diamond & Dybvig highlight the potential for
a bank run that triggers a broad crisis, empirical research shows that in practice, it
is exceedingly rare for a bank run to lead to bank failure in the absence of an
underlying solvency issue (Correia, Luck and Verner 2026). In other words, the

presence of amplification channels alone does not lead to a crisis.
Conversely, in the absence of amplification, it is possible to get significant
turbulence in asset prices, but without a financial crisis. For instance, a bear market,
wider credit spreads, or even a recession do not in and of themselves imply there
is a financial crisis. This was seen clearly with the bursting of the dot com bubble,
a severe equity decline but not a financial crisis, though it did cause a recession. At

the same time, in the chart below, we can see several US equity bear markets that
took place outside of recessions. On other occasions, however, a “Minsky
Moment” can then happen, where even a seemingly minor event can trigger a high-
profile incident that cascades across the financial system.
Figure 1: S&P 500 drawdowns over time, with shaded recessions showing select episodes when the index fell over 20%
without coinciding with a recession
Source :Bloomberg Finance LP, NBER, Deutsche Bank Research

Looking back through history, we can see how variation in these factors has led to
different regimes over time in terms of frequency/severity of financial factors.

3 November 2026
Thematic Research
Page 4 Deutsche Bank AG
Section 2. Why have financial crises been more or less likely
in certain periods of history?
1. Pre-WWII: More frequent financial instability, albeit under a very
different system.
Financial instability was a recurrent feature of the pre-WWII era. During the 19th
and early 20th centuries, there were frequent panics that often led to major slumps.
For instance, the Panic of 1873 sparked a period often known as the “Long

Depression”, which extended into the 1890s for many countries.
In part, financial panics occurred more frequently because policy operated under
very different settings. For instance, the Gold Standard as the monetary system
constrained a countercyclical policy response and transmitted deflation
internationally, while falling prices increased real debt burdens. Moreover, the
Federal Reserve wasn’t established until 1913, and the lack of an effective lender of

last resort meant there was a private intervention during the US Panic of 1907. The
first wave of globalisation also brought new vulnerabilities. Between 1870 and
1913, many emerging economies accumulated debts denominated in gold or
foreign currency, leaving them exposed when capital inflows reversed or exchange
rates weakened.
The biggest and most famous crisis occurred in 1929, when the Wall Street Crash
of that year sparked the Great Depression. The length and depth of that crisis helped

to stimulate the study of modern macroeconomics, with huge efforts to prevent a
repeat over the years ahead. Indeed, the failures of the response to the Great
Depression played a large role in the much swifter response to the 2008 financial
crisis, helping to avoid a second Great Depression.
That experience established much of the modern financial safety net that exists to
this day. For instance in the US, the Federal Deposit Insurance Corporation was

established in 1933. The US and many other countries came off the Gold Standard
as well in this period. There were also regulatory reforms, and the Glass-Steagall Act
separated out commercial from investment banking.
2. 1944-1971: The Bretton Woods era coincided with fewer financial
crises
Around the end of WWII, economic policymakers sought to create an international
monetary system for the post-war era. The relative financial calm that followed

reflected a combination of restrictions on international capital movement, tightly
regulated domestic financial systems, and a system of fixed exchange rates that
aimed to provide stability and prevent the competitive devaluations that occurred
in the 1930s. This saw exchange rates pegged against the US Dollar, which in turn
was convertible to gold at $35 per ounce.
At the same time, capital controls helped preserve monetary policy flexibility, while

maintaining the system of fixed exchange rates. These controls had several
downsides but they also insulated countries from “sudden stop” crises that can
occur, when capital rapidly flees a country. Moreover, capital controls prevented
the rapid buildup of cross-border systemic leverage. So it was more difficult to
generate a global financial crisis in the same way.

Alongside capital controls, there were also domestic reforms in the aftermath of the
Great Depression, which sought to avoid a repeat. As mentioned earlier, the US
Glass-Steagall Act of 1933 separated commercial banking from investment

banking. Another example of the era’s regulated financial system in the US was
Regulation Q, which imposed maximum interest rates that commercial banks
could pay on deposits. That meant depositors could not search for yield and cause
a run on deposits as easily. Another rationale was that with institutions not
competing for deposits, it would avoid a compression in the margin between the
rates banks paid on their lending and borrowing, which would disincentivise a push

into riskier investments.
Finally, economic growth was also stronger in this period, with the period between
WWII and the 1973 oil shock often described as the “Golden Age”. This played a role
in avoiding financial crises too, as faster growth helped keep debt default rates
down, avoiding the kind of “trigger” events above that might spark a financial crisis.
Coupled with financial repression, strong growth also helped to bring down the
high post-war public debt burdens.

Over time however, the peg between the dollar and gold found itself under growing
strain. The economist Robert Triffin identified the “Triffin dilemma”. Supplying the
world with dollar reserves resulted in outstanding dollar liabilities around the world
that exceeded the gold reserves in the US, weakening confidence that dollars could
ultimately be redeemed for gold. Conversely, restricting dollar supply to protect
those reserves and preserve convertibility risked depriving the system of the

liquidity needed to support trade and growth.
These tensions intensified in the late 1960s amid expansionary US policies,
including spending on the Vietnam War and Great Society programmes. The
continued drain in US gold reserves eventually led the US to suspend the
convertibility of the US Dollar into gold, effectively ending the Bretton Woods
system. Initially, there were attempts to bring some kind of fixed-exchange rate
system back, but the world of fiat currencies (with no link to a precious metal)

persists to this day.

### Page 6

3 November 2026
Thematic Research
Page 6 Deutsche Bank AG
3. 1971-2008: Financial crises become more frequent after the
breakdown of Bretton Woods to the Global Financial Crisis
After the breakdown of the Bretton Woods system, financial crises became a more
regular occurrence. While the end of the gold convertibility did not by itself trigger
future crises, it opened the door for wider financial liberalisation, with the resulting
rise in capital mobility and looser domestic financial regulation then helping to

facilitate a global build-up of imbalances.
The 1980s and 1990s were a period of frequent crisis events. The transition away
from capital controls meant that capital could flow more freely across borders.
However, this could also cause a “sudden stop” if investors suddenly pulled their
capital out of a country, contributing to bank runs and currency collapses.
Moreover, many emerging markets borrowed in foreign currencies like the US
Dollar, meaning that a depreciation in their own currency would see this USD-

denominated debt surge in local currency terms. This meant it was a particular
problem if US interest rates were increasing, as it became harder for many
emerging markets to repay their debts. This was a contributing factor behind the
Latin American debt crisis in the early 1980s. And the rate hikes also had domestic
ramifications, being a contributing factor behind the Savings and Loan crisis in the
United States.
With capital flowing more freely, that also played a role in the 1994 Mexican Peso

Crisis, and the 1997 Asian Financial Crisis. A sudden reversal in capital flows led to
crisis and ultimately recession in many countries. The following year, the Russian
financial crisis then took place, and fears grew in the United States about the wider
ramifications, as the hedge fund Long-Term Capital Management (LTCM) came
under pressure. Indeed, this period saw 75bps of pre-emptive rate cuts from the
Federal Reserve.
This volatility also saw the Fed proactively seek to step in and smooth out the

economic volatility. This was broadly successful, but in the process also helped
engender complacency and a further buildup of leverage. Indeed, after the dot com
bubble burst, the federal funds rate was cut to just 1% in mid-2003, where it
remained until mid-2004.
This period saw clear optimism that another Great Depression was highly unlikely.
For instance, Nobel Prize-winning economist Robert Lucas argued in his 2003 AEA
Presidential Address that “macroeconomics in this original sense has succeeded:

Its central problem of depression prevention has been solved, for all practical
purposes, and has in fact been solved for many decades.”
However, as we now know, the 2008 financial crisis occurred, driving the biggest
global downturn since the Great Depression of the 1930s. It turned out that Minsky
had been correct, and a lengthy period without a crisis had once again helped to
induce complacency, laying the foundations for a repeat.

Section 3: The Post-2008 world: Why have we not had
another big financial crisis?
Since the Global Financial Crisis of 2008, we have thankfully avoided a major
financial crisis. Despite clear moments of stress, most notably around the collapse
of SVB in March 2023, we have not seen a similar wave of bank collapses on that
scale. There are several components to this.
First, post-GFC deleveraging cleared out risks in the system, with countries and
sectors that had excess indebtedness before the GFC experiencing pronounced

declines in leverage.
Second, there have been reforms explicitly designed to avoid a repeat of 2008.
After the crisis happened, there was a clear recognition that gaps in regulation had
exacerbated the crisis, leading to effects that cascaded across the financial system.
For instance, the Basel III reforms required banks to hold more equity, with a
minimum requirement for Common Equity Tier 1 (CET1) capital. They also
introduced the liquidity coverage ratio, which requires banks to hold enough high-

quality liquid assets to ensure they can cover their net cash outflows under a 30-day
stressed scenario. In addition, the net stable funding ratio was brought in, requiring
banks to have sufficient stable funding that exceeded the required amount of stable
funding for a one-year stressed scenario.
At the national level, further reforms were also introduced. In the US, the Dodd-
Frank Act was introduced, with measures including the Volcker Rule that restricted

banks from engaging in proprietary trading, and required the Federal Reserve to
perform an annual stress test of large institutions. Meanwhile in the European
Union, which also experienced a sovereign debt crisis, reforms included a “Single
Supervisory Mechanism”, whereby banking supervision was brought under the
ECB, ensuring harmonised regulatory standards.

### Page 8

3 November 2026
Thematic Research
Page 8 Deutsche Bank AG
Third, the share of financial assets held by banks has fallen since the 2008 crisis.
This hasn’t eliminated risk, but commercial banks are funded by retail deposits that
can quickly fly. By contrast, non-bank financial institutions (NBFIs), e.g. hedge
funds, are not as prone to these bank run dynamics. For instance, a private credit
closed-ended fund is not one where investors can simply redeem their funds on

demand.
We can see this shift in the data. Looking at the FSB’s group of 21 plus the Eurozone,
we can see that the share of assets held by banks has fallen from a peak of 46% in
2008, to 38% in 2024. By contrast, the share held by NBFIs has risen from 43% to
51%.
Figure 5: Share of Assets of Financial Corporations (FSB 21 + EA Group)

Source :Haver Analytics, Deutsche Bank

Fourth, the lessons of 2008 have meant that when the financial system has had
wobbles since, policymakers have acted with incredible speed to stop the
turmoil.
This was most obvious when the Covid-19 pandemic began. Whilst a pandemic
was very different to a financial crisis, there was initially huge turmoil in financial
markets. Indeed, the S&P 500 fell by more than a third in just over a month, a decline
at a speed and scale unseen since the Great Depression. Meanwhile, US HY credit

spreads soared to levels last seen around the financial crisis, and at the height of the
turmoil, there was a huge spike in real yields, despite the economy entering a
recession.
Against this backdrop, central banks wasted no time in acting. For instance, the Fed
delivered an emergency 50bp rate cut, followed by another 100bp rate cut.
Moreover, they swiftly resumed Quantitative Easing, and announced the
establishment of multiple new facilities to ensure the smooth functioning of credit

markets. These included a Primary Market Corporate Credit Facility, to support new
bond and loan issuance, along with a Secondary Market Corporate Credit facility,
which offered liquidity for outstanding corporate bonds. Meanwhile, a third facility,
called the Term Asset-Backed Securities Loan Facility, was set up to enable the
issuance of asset-backed securities. Similarly in Europe, the ECB set up a new
Pandemic Emergency Purchase Programme (PEPP), which ultimately had a total

envelope of €1.85tn.
Whilst the pandemic caused a huge economic shock, the swift action meant it did
not translate into a financial one, and the brief tightening in credit conditions was

### Page 9

3 November 2026
Thematic Research
Deutsche Bank AG Page 9
unwound over the coming months.
A similar pattern was seen during the regional banking turmoil of March 2023. This
was a financial panic, which initially began with the collapse of Silicon Valley Bank.
However, this began to spread, and Signature Bank also failed. Then in Europe,
Credit Suisse came under investor scrutiny and saw large deposit outflows, which
culminated in a purchase by UBS that included guarantees from the Swiss

government. This coincided with huge market moves, with the 2yr Treasury yield
posting its biggest daily decline in over 40 years, with a 61bps decline on March 13.
However, policymakers swiftly moved to stop any contagion. The US Treasury,
Federal Reserve and the FDIC released a joint statement stating that all deposits at
SVB would be protected, and that the Federal Reserve would provide additional
funding to ensure banks could meet the needs of depositors. Meanwhile, the Fed’s

balance sheet briefly expanded again, despite the fact it had begun the process of
quantitative tightening. These actions ultimately meant the crisis proved contained
and did not lead to a wave of further bank collapses in the manner of 2008.

Fifth, policymakers have also responded to recent shocks with huge fiscal

transfers, meaning that debt has migrated from private towards sovereign
balance sheets.
Whilst global debt has risen slightly over the last decade, if you dig deeper into the
numbers, that’s entirely driven by a rise in government debt. If we compare the
statistics for Q2 2026, relative to Q2 2016, there’s actually been a decline in debt for
households, financial corporations, and nonfinancial corporations. The only one of
the four segments that’s gone up in the last decade is government debt.

In relative terms, the transformation is even bigger if we look back to 2007, before
the Global Financial Crisis kicked off. Back then, government debt was the smallest
of the four categories, but because of the GFC and the pandemic, it’s now become
the biggest of the four categories.
That in turn has led to separate issues, with government bond yields at multi-year
highs, alongside rising net interest spending. So the fiscal space has become
limited. However, public balance sheets don’t face the same kind of system-wide

run that a bank would.

Overall, the post-2008 experience offers three key forward-looking lessons. First,
stronger bank balance sheets and the shift towards non-bank finance suggest

future stress may originate outside banks. As such, historical analyses centred on
banking crises may provide a less complete guide. Second, policymakers have
become increasingly effective at containing liquidity pressures and amplification.
And third, the shift in debt towards sovereign balance sheets has cushioned private-
sector shocks but narrowed the capacity for future intervention.
Section 4: What are potential candidates for the next crisis?

This section considers some of the current risks in the financial system and whether
they could be sowing the seeds for the next financial crisis. This section is not
designed to be a comprehensive list of financial risks, but rather an assessment of
three important areas where risks have risen in recent years.
1. Could AI spark broader vulnerabilities?
A huge driver of the current equity bull run has been the growth in AI-related stocks.
This report won’t explore the debate as to whether this is a bubble, but examine

whether a correction could eventually propagate into a systemic financial crisis. As
noted earlier, a reversal in the investment cycle need not cause a crisis in itself, and
the bursting of the dot com bubble also did not lead to a broader financial crisis.
Nevertheless, there are issues that could spark vulnerabilities. First, the rapid rise
in AI capex raises questions about whether the investment will pay off. The AI build-
out is on course to exceed the scale of past technological investment cycles, which

have often involved overinvestment even when the technologies ultimately proved
transformative. Second, funding is shifting from predominantly operating cash
flow towards external capital, and rising debt issuance in particular. Third, greater
equity-market concentration increases exposure to a reassessment of technology
valuations. Together, these developments make a correction a clear risk.
To assess the potential for systemic stress, we apply the framework in Greenwood,

Hanson, Shleifer and Sørensen (GHSS). This associates heightened crisis risk with
the combination of a strong run-up in asset prices and excessive credit growth,
estimating a 45% probability of a crisis within 3 years if the two thresholds in Figure
11 are met. On a backward-looking basis as of 2025, an economy-wide assessment
finds that the equity-price increase meets the framework’s “red zone” test, but
business credit growth has been considerably more subdued, given the slowdown

### Page 11

3 November 2026
Thematic Research
Deutsche Bank AG Page 11
in credit following the 2022-23 tightening cycle. That is, going into 2026, the pattern
was one of a displacement of credit from other interest-sensitive sectors towards
AI and technology rather than any broad-based rise in leverage.
Nevertheless, US data does show a clear acceleration in credit trends so far in 2026.
To make a more forward-looking assessment, we incorporate the rise in issuance

in 2026 and indicatively extend it into 2027 using the estimated rise in AI capex
plans, assuming credit growth elsewhere in the US economy remains stable
(footnote: We see two-sided risks to this – we may understate the broader credit
demand in tech adjacent areas; conversely, this may understate the drag of higher
rates on credit demand in non-AI related sectors). We also extend the estimated
2027 trend into 2028, including an assumption that equity prices continue their

trend over the past 12 months, to provide an illustrative scenario over the next two
years. Under these assumptions, we would see a visible rise in aggregate US
business sector leverage, leaving the sector just shy of economy-wide red-zone
levels by 2028. As such, approaching that territory by 2028 is conceivable if the AI
cycle continues to accelerate, both in terms of increased debt borrowing and a
renewed rise in valuations.
While the GHSS framework gives a useful quantification of the potential risks, its

indicators also warrant some qualification, which we judge to come in opposing
directions:
n On the one hand, there is a risk that aggregate credit measures may
understate vulnerabilities when borrowing is highly concentrated. Circular
financing, leases and off-balance-sheet structures complicate the picture,
and while we incorporate the recent rise in payables in our leverage metric
to capture some of this risk, it may well be underestimated. Moreover,

realised interest costs remain below current market borrowing rates after
the lengthy period of ultra-low rates, so leverage trends alone may miss the
pressure from rising debt-service costs.
n On the other hand, the price-based metric used by GHSS to assess excess
asset price growth (the 3-year real rise in equity prices) may overstate
valuation excesses given the strong rise in earnings. Indeed, P/E multiples
have already seen a sizeable correction since late 2025, which was most

pronounced for software stocks, and currently there is little evidence of
overly stretched tech valuations, especially when compared to the dot com
boom.
The risk of stress amplification is also likely to depend on the nature of any AI
correction. A downgrading of the AI capex cycle due to slower growth in AI
demand, financing constraints or supply bottlenecks may result in weaker
valuations, leading to negative wealth effects and slower capital expenditure.

However, while this would create an economic headwind, it need not produce a
systemic crisis. And while parallels to the dot com bubble may well be relevant,
currently there are factors that suggest downside risks would be more moderate.
For instance, operating cash flow still plays an important role in financing the AI
buildout, whilst there has been a limited run-up in borrowing and much less
stretched valuations relative to the late-1990s.
Our assessment therefore remains that an AI correction is a clearer risk than an AI-

led systemic crisis at this point. For these risks to become more material, it would
need leverage to keep building but AI returns to eventually disappoint, raising the
likelihood of credit events and amplifying the investment slowdown through the
real economy. We will focus on this interaction between debt-service pressures and

the role of credit events more in the next section.

2. Risks from higher rates and private credit
In the previous section we noted that modest economy-wide debt growth doesn’t
necessarily imply modest credit risk when debt-service costs are rising. As existing
debt reprices or is refinanced, borrowers can face greater pressure even without
additional borrowing. This is a key feature of the debt cycle, whereby vulnerabilities
accumulate during the expansion, and rising rates (often amid a monetary

tightening due to an overheating economy) can then expose them. These risks are
relevant in the context of the current rate hiking cycle, especially as much of the
catch up in debt service costs is yet to play out (see chart below).
With banking-sector risks having moderated since the GFC, the rapid expansion of
private credit and its opaque structure have understandably drawn attention as a
possible source of the next crisis. Parallels with the subprime credit boom have

been periodically made. However, we think these should not be overstated. Even
though the dollar size of the US private credit market is similar to that of subprime
around 2006-07, the market must be assessed relative to the economy (which has
doubled in nominal terms). Moreover, the slower build-up, restrictions on
redemptions and lower risks of direct wealth effects all suggest much more
moderate amplification risks than in 2008.
There are already signs that the private-credit growth cycle is slowing (see chart)

and our credit strategists have noted warning signs including weaker new inflows
into BDCs, increasing payment-in-kind interest and prospective refinancing
pressures. That said, defaults remain limited thus far so these should not be seen
as evidence of an imminent systemic event.

### Page 14

3 November 2026
Thematic Research
Page 14 Deutsche Bank AG
Whether more systemic risks could emerge will depend on issues of solvency,
opacity and capacity for intervention.
n First, while redemption gates can limit immediate cash outflows and
reduce the need to sell illiquid loans under pressure, they do not restore a
borrower’s ability to service debt or absorb underlying credit losses. This
distinction between liquidity protection and solvency is therefore central to

judging whether a slowing credit cycle remains orderly.
n Opacity remains a concern, both in financing structures and in the
distribution of exposures among end-holders, including insurers and other
non-banks. This can make losses harder to identify and potential
amplification harder to assess.
n Policymakers’ success in containing liquidity pressures may not translate
into an effective response to credit events - liquidity provision alone cannot

repair impaired balance sheets. Extending support to opaque non-bank
exposures raises difficult questions about eligibility, loss allocation and the
threshold for intervention.
Quite aside from private credit, the fact that we are back in a rate hiking cycle from
the major central banks also adds to the current risks. Indeed, higher rates bring
down inflation, in part because they tighten financial conditions. But a
consequence of this is that it often reveals vulnerabilities in the financial system,

and makes a crisis more likely. For instance, it was a contributing factor behind the
2023 regional banking crisis, as banks had growing unrealised losses on their
securities portfolio when rates began to rise again. So even if higher rates don’t
directly cause a crisis, they can often be a key contributor. Indeed, hiking cycles
have regularly coincided with some kind of shock, as the experience of recent
decades demonstrates.

3. Stretched fiscal positions and possible NBFI amplifiers
As discussed earlier, the migration of debt towards sovereign balance sheets has

helped cushion private-sector shocks, but this has come at the cost of higher fiscal
risks. Higher public debt and sovereign yields have limited the room for fiscal
stimulus, while persistent inflation has limited the room for monetary stimulus. This
makes another round of coordinated macroeconomic support harder to deliver.
The issue with high public debt is that it if fiscal sustainability is questioned, that can
raise sovereign borrowing costs, which can amplify existing private-sector

vulnerabilities via a tightening in financial conditions and slower growth.
The financial risk posed by sovereign stress will also depend on the financial
amplification channels. These were clear in the European sovereign crisis with the
bank-sovereign loop, but the changing structure of sovereign markets suggests
that the sovereign–NBFI feedback loop may now warrant increasing attention. This
was visible with the 2022 LDI crisis in the UK, while a more recent concern is the run

up in leveraged hedge fund positions in sovereign bond markets, with hedge funds’
sovereign bond exposures more than doubling since 2022 (see chart below). As
noted also by the IMF, this reflects greater issuance and a rising share of sovereign
debt held by hedge funds.
The lessons from Black Monday, LTCM and the LDI crisis offer reassurance that
intervention can contain market stress originating outside the banking system. But
they are not a guarantee that future liquidity shocks will remain economically

benign. The greater scale and interconnectedness of non-bank finance mean that
we can’t simply rely on historical examples today.
Looking forward, a combination of sovereign repricing, leveraged financial
amplification and a more constrained policy response risks proving harder to
resolve than past episodes.

Conclusion
Whilst a major financial crisis has been avoided since the GFC and the subsequent
sovereign crisis, the problem of financial instability has yet to be permanently

solved. On the one hand, reforms to the financial system and the swift actions of
policymakers have helped to prevent a repeat. But as we know from Hyman
Minsky’s warning, lengthy periods of stability can often lay the foundations for the
next crisis, even if these take place slowly. Indeed, risks often shift rather than
disappear.
Looking forward, there are several vectors of risk worth paying attention to. But
financial crises often start in areas where people are paying little attention, or risks

are assumed to be contained. Indeed, it is the areas where risk is underappreciated
which often prove the most vulnerable.
As such, predicting where the next crisis might arise is inherently difficult. But the
topic is a vital one, given the long-lasting damage that financial crises can cause
when they do occur. Indeed, with the fiscal space for sovereigns increasingly
constrained today, the margin for a future policy error is limited.

What’s New – RTX Spark 高階筆電需求強勁 
NVIDIA RTX Spark正式開賣，華碩、Dell、HP、聯想、Microsoft 與微星等六家品牌共推出
九款筆電，美國起售價 US$2,599.99起，台灣 128GB 版本售價逾 NT$23 萬。RTX Spark 最
高搭載 128GB Unified Memory，可於本地端執行 1,200 億參數 LLM。儘管定價遠高於一般
高階筆電，華碩首波 24GB 至 128GB 各規格 產品到貨即售罄，微星亦向 NVIDIA 追加晶片配
額，主要買家為企業、AI 開發者與內容創作者。企業目標由本地設備處理約 80%的基礎工作
以降低 Token 成本，顯示高算力、大容量 Unified Memory 的本地 Agent 平台已有明確客群。 
Trends/Outlook – 觸控 MacBook Pro 推升高階 ASP 
Apple 近期推出首款觸控螢幕 MacBook Pro，採 Tandem OLED 的 PROLED XDR 面板，14 
吋／16 吋起售價 US$2,899／3,399，較同晶片 mini-LED 機型高 US$400；M6 MacBook 
Pro 則維持 US$1,999 起，較 2025 年 M5 上市價高出 25.0%。M6 的 170GB/s 頻寬僅限 
24GB 以上版本，顯示 Apple 將 Local AI 效能集中於高容量配置，引導升級記憶體，顯示
Apple以新增高價位產品線推升產品組合。綜觀整體市場，3Q26 全球 PC 出貨 YoY -20.1%
（IDC 初估），記憶體合約價漲幅雖收斂但仍持續上揚，Intel、AMD 與華碩皆預期 2027 年 
PC 出貨持續衰退。研究部預期記憶體價格將居高不下，可能要到 4Q27 才會見到下滑，市場
「總量偏弱、高階 AI PC 強」的分化現象將持續。 
Investment Summary 
1. RTX Spark 與觸控 MacBook Pro 上市，推升高階筆電定價區間，兩者均鎖定價格敏感
度低的企業、AI 開發者與內容創作者。隨本地端執行 AI Agent 可降低雲端 Token 支出，
大容量 Unified Memory 成為高階機種主要規格，品牌廠成長動能將由出貨量轉向 ASP、
單機 Memory Content 與產品組合，3Q26 華碩廣義 AI PC 營收 YoY 逾 +100%、Intel 
AI PC 營收 YoY +35%，已反映此一趨勢。AMD 新平台亦支援最高 192GB Unified 
Memory，競爭焦點已由 NPU 轉向整機架構。 
2. 華碩 3Q26 PC 出貨年減 8.6%，PC 營收仍年增逾 25%，顯示產品組合升級可有效抵銷
出貨下滑。具備 RTX Spark 首波產品、高階機種占比高及 AI 伺服器業務的品牌廠，營
運仍具韌性。看好華碩(2357 TT)，其 ProArt P14／P16 為 RTX Spark 機種占比擴大，
加上伺服器營收強勁貢獻，營運維持成長；另正向看待微星(2377 TT)等品牌廠，其已將 
RTX Spark 與高階機種導入筆電、桌機與工作站，有利整機 ASP 與產品組合持續優化。

2 
RTX Spark 高階筆電需求強勁 
NVIDIA RTX Spark 筆電近期正式開賣，華碩(2357 TT)、Dell(DELL US)、HP(HPQ US)、聯想
(0992 HK)、Microsoft(MSFT US)與微星(2377 TT)等六家品牌皆分別推出相關機種，為 NVIDIA首
度以自家 Grace CPU搭配 Blackwell RTX GPU的完整平台導入 Windows 11筆電。RTX Spark 
N1X 分為兩種配置：高階版本整合 20 核心 Grace CPU、6,144 核心 Blackwell RTX GPU 及
24~128GB Unified Memory，適用筆電與小型桌機；入門版本則為 18 核心 CPU、5,120 核心
GPU及 24GB／32GB Unified Memory，對應各品牌 US$2,599.99起之入門機種。高階版本最高
具備 1 Petaflop FP4算力，可於本地端執行 1,200億參數 LLM。 
表一：RTX Spark 首波主要筆電機種美國起售價 
品牌 機種 起售價 
Dell XPS 16 US$3,799.99 
微星 Prestige N16 Flip AI+ US$3,299.99 
HP OmniBook Ultra 16 US$3,199.99 
聯想 Yoga 9n US$3,099.99 
HP OmniBook X 14 US$2,999.99 
華碩 ProArt P16 US$2,799.99 
華碩 ProArt P14 US$2,599.99 
Microsoft Surface Laptop Ultra US$2,599.99 
資料來源：國泰證期研究部整理 
台灣市場方面，華碩 ProArt P14／P16 128GB版本售價分別為 NT$25萬／27萬，64GB版本自
NT$17萬／18萬起；微星 Prestige N16 Flip AI+ 128GB版本售價 NT$24萬、64GB版本 NT$14
萬。高階主力機種售價皆逾 NT$20萬，市場原先對首波需求偏向保守。 
然而實際需求卻優於預期。華碩首波 24GB 至 128GB 各規格產品到貨後即迅速售罄，全球需求強
勁，短期出現供應瓶頸；微星首批筆電開放預購後即售罄，已向 NVIDIA 爭取更多晶片配額。
NVIDIA也表示，產品價格雖高但需求強勁，並點名開發 AI服務與應用的企業為重要需求來源。從
客群來看，首波買家主要為內容創作者及有地端 AI 開發需求的企業。顯示企業正採取「地端先運
算、雲端再精修」的 Hybrid 架構，由本地設備處理約 80~90%的基礎工作，再將需進一步處理的
內容送往雲端，以降低 Token 成本並兼顧資料隱私。首波訂單主要來自企業與 AI 開發者等高價值
客群，對價格敏感度低且具明確的本地 AI需求，有助支撐品牌廠高階產品組合與毛利率。隨 11月
第二批產品出貨、64GB版本放量，以及 1Q27中低階 N1版本推出，RTX Spark可望由專業用戶
逐步擴大至高階主流市場。軟體生態亦同步到位，Adobe已針對 Photoshop、Premiere等軟體進
行最佳化，隨 Windows on Arm相容性持續改善，將進一步擴大消費客群。

### Page 3

投資 顧問 
產業報告

2026/11/17

3 
蘋果推出觸控 MacBook Pro，以新增高階產品線拉高 ASP 
蘋果(AAPL US)上月發表首款觸控螢幕 Mac，搭載 Tandem OLED 面板(PROLED XDR)、採 M5 
Pro／M5 Max晶片的 MacBook Pro，14吋與 16吋起售價分別為 US$2,899與 US$3,399。觸控
機型並未取代既有產品，而是定位於同晶片 mini-LED 機型之上，起始配置相同下售價高出
US$400。蘋果也推出 14吋 M6 MacBook Pro，採 2奈米製程的 M6晶片，Neural Engine由單
組 16核心升級為 Dual 16-core，記憶體頻寬由 153GB/s提升至 170GB/s。 
圖一、圖二：蘋果 MacBook Pro with PROLED XDR 
資料來源：Apple、國泰證期研究部整理 
M6 MacBook Pro起售價 US$1,999，雖與調漲後的 M5機型相同，但較 M5上市價 US$1,599高
出 25.0%，且起始配置仍為 16GB。170GB/s頻寬僅限 24GB與 32GB版本，蘋果主要效能宣稱亦
以 32GB版本測得，顯示蘋果在記憶體成本高漲下，選擇保留基本款規格，將 Local AI效能差異集
中於高容量配置，引導使用者升級記憶體。搭配觸控 OLED 新產品線，蘋果的定價策略由「既有產
品漲價」轉向「新增更高價位產品」，有利整體 ASP提升。

PC 總量下滑，高階 AI PC 營收逆勢成長 
3Q26全球 PC市場未見傳統旺季效應。單季出貨 6,205萬台(-9.0% QoQ，-19.9% YoY)，連續兩
季年減，主要原因在於 1H26 品牌廠與通路提前備貨、終端售價高檔及零組件供給受限三者疊加。
然而高階與 AI機種表現明顯優於市場平均。華碩 3Q26 PC出貨 550萬台(-8.6% YoY)，PC營收年
增逾 25%，其中內含 NPU的 AI PC營收年增逾 100%，占 PC營收 53%；電競、高階、AI PC與
商用 PC 等高價值產品合計占比 65%。華碩 4Q26 指引伺服器營收季增 150%，PC 營收則預期季
減 15%。蘋果 3Q26 PC 出貨年減 11.3%，同期 Mac 營收卻成長 23.7%。Intel CCPG(Client 
Computing and Physical AI Group)營收 US$8.1bn(-5% YoY)，AI PC營收卻年增 35%。 
綜上所述，出貨量與營收的落差主要來自 ASP 上升與產品組合轉向高階。RTX Spark、AMD 
Ryzen AI Max PRO 400系列(最高 192GB)及蘋果 M系列皆以大容量 Unified Memory支援本地
大型模型，AI PC競爭焦點已由 NPU TOPS轉向 CPU、GPU與記憶體的整機架構。

### Page 4

投資 顧問 
產業報告

2026/11/17

4 
記憶體價格居高不下，結構性分化將持續 
記憶體合約價漲幅雖收斂，但尚未出現反轉訊號。供給面方面，原廠持續將先進製程產能投入
Server應用，27年 PC DRAM供給可能進一步縮減；PC OEM考量 27年供給吃緊，4Q26仍維持
積極採購。成本壓力已反映於各廠對 27年 PC市場的展望。預估 26年產業 PC出貨約年減 12%、
27 年約年減 10%，並指引 4Q26 CCPG 營收季減逾 20%；華碩預估 27 年全球 PC 市場萎縮
10~15%，上半年需求偏弱、下半年才逐步回升。在 DRAM 與 CPU 價格維持高檔下，為壓低
BOM成本，品牌廠已調降主流機種 SSD容量，中低階機種面臨「降規漲價」壓力。 
綜合上述，研究部預期記憶體價格將居高不下，可能要到 4Q27 才會見到下滑，短期仍將對 PC 市
場造成壓力，尤以價格敏感的消費型與中低階機種影響最大。反觀 RTX Spark 與觸控 MacBook 
Pro等高階產品，主要客群為企業、AI開發者與內容創作者，對價格敏感度較低，且本地端執行 AI 
Agent可降低雲端 Token支出，需求較具韌性。整體而言，PC市場「總量偏弱、高階 AI PC較強」
的結構性分化將持續，產業動能將由出貨量擴張轉向 ASP、Memory Content 與產品組合升級，
具備高階 AI PC產品線與定價能力的品牌廠，可望持續擴大與同業的營運差距。

重點摘要
1
分析師／林亞瑱
研究範疇／歐洲總經政策與市場分析
《財政與政治不確定性壓抑估值，歐股延續震盪》
市場對法國財政可持續性以及其可能產生的外溢擔
憂升溫，牽動十月以來歐股走勢震盪，銀行股跌勢
較重，所幸食品飲料與健護等防禦型產業、能源類
股上揚支撐歐股收紅。評估目前情勢屬法國財政信
心危機，而非歐洲主權風險全面升溫；融資成本上
升對歐洲企業帶來壓力，然整體槓桿可控，財務體
質脆弱的企業面臨較大風險。隨著預算協商進行、
財政擔憂短線難消除，歐央12月利率會議可能再升
息一碼，公債殖利率預估將維持高位，加上中東地
緣風險未除，將對市場情緒與歐股評價帶來壓力，
預估歐股短線將延續震盪走勢，維持中立評等。

• 法國財政疑慮致法德利差大
幅擴大。
• 預估現任總理勒克努仍有機
會透過援引《憲法》第49.3
條於年底前通過一份財政整

而，即使預算案過關，也難
扭轉法國債務攀升趨勢。
資料來源：野村，2026/10/7，中國信託商業銀行彙整，2026/10。
2027年預算提案樽節開支，但將面對政治角力
《法國財政疑慮、政治僵局與社會動盪導致法德利
差擴大，明年大選前政治風險溢酬預估維持高檔》
十月份法國公債殖利率大幅上升，反映法國政治
局勢分裂、財政整頓前景不明，加上明年總統大
選恐由極右派勝出，市場對法國財政可持續性的
疑慮升高，法國十年期公債殖利率10月下旬一度
突破5%，創2002年7月以來新高。

近期法國各地爆發大規模抗議活動，亦對市場帶
來衝擊。法國政治與財政風險升溫，反而吸引資
金轉向德國公債避險，加上近期金融條件趨緊、
市場對歐央升息的預期相較九月底降溫，推動德
國十年期公債殖利率自高檔回落，法德利差大幅
擴大、10/22一度突破170個基點，創2011年11
月歐債危機以來新高(統計至10/26)。
針對2027年預算，法國政府提出430億歐元財政
整頓方案，目標將財政赤字占GDP比率自2026年
的5.4%降至2027年的5%，措施包括凍結公部門
薪資、限制每月超過1,260歐元的退休金通膨調整
幅度、延長針對大型企業的特別稅但縮小適用範
圍等。反對黨已對多項措施表達反對，若不採取
任何樽節措施，2027年法國財政赤字占GDP比率
預估將達6.5%。由於大選在即，從近期極右派國
民聯盟釋出的意向來看，雖不支持預算案，卻也
不願在大選前引發政治危機，預估現任總理勒克
努仍有機會透過援引《憲法》第49.3條於年底前
通過一份財政整頓力度較有限的預算案。然而，
即使預算案過關，也難扭轉法國債務攀升趨勢。

重點摘要
3
近來融資成本上升進一步加重法國的債務負擔。
信評機構惠譽(2026/10/12)上調利息支出預測，
預估2026年至2028年間，利息支出將累積佔GDP
的0.8%。利息支出占GDP比率預估將由2026年約
2.5%升至2028年約3.3%，2030年進一步升至
3.8%，反映高融資成本對財政的壓力將逐步顯現。
利息支出增加將壓縮其他公共支出及財政空間。
若赤字改善幅度不如預期，債務比率恐持續上升。
2027年總統大選亦將牽動法國財政政策與債市走
向。法國總統大選預計於2027年4至5月舉行，第
一輪投票訂於4/18，若無候選人取得過半選票將
於5/2進行第二輪投票。最新民調顯示，極右派國
民聯盟候選人勒龐在首輪支持度領先，超過三成；

極左派法國不屈黨候選人梅朗雄近期支持度上升，
引發市場擔憂將發生沒有中間派候選人進入第二
輪決選的不利情境。
梅朗雄主張擴張性財政政策，對市場最為不利，
勝選機率較高的勒龐10/6提出財政整頓方案並且
主張建立一個可信的債務穩定框架，試圖安撫市
場對法國財政前景的疑慮。在面對市場壓力下，
勒龐表達更具財政紀律的立場有助稍稍緩和財政
擴張的擔憂，但其財政整頓方案的政策假設與具
體實施存在不確定性，加上大選後很可能緊接著
進行國會改選，政治版圖維持分裂狀態不利財政
改革。
資料來源：Fitch，2026/10/12，中國信託商業銀行彙整，2026/10。

重點摘要
4
整體而言，財政和政治不確定性預估將令法德利
差維持在高位，下檔情境為若2027年預算案無法
於年底前通過，恐將面臨更大的財政惡化與市場
壓力，十年期法德利差恐有進一步擴大的風險。
《目前情勢屬法國財政信心危機，而非歐洲主權風
險全面升溫》
儘管投資人對法國債券要求更高的風險補償，但
近期法國公債標售仍有足夠的認購需求。除此之
外，目前法德利差擴大對整體歐洲市場的外溢影
響尚屬可控，多數歐洲國家相對德國利差雖受牽
動近期亦出現較明顯的擴大，但利差水準仍可控。
以政府債務占GDP比重較高的義大利來看，十年
期義德利差10/22一度升至124個基點，回升至
2025年4月水準，但仍遠低於2011年歐債危機時
期。因此，目前情勢仍偏向法國自身的財政信心

危機，而非歐洲主權信用風險全面升溫。
資料來源：Bloomberg，2024年來至2026/10/21，中國信託商業銀行彙整，2026/10。

《歐元區PMI維持擴張但成長放緩，通膨壓力面臨
上行風險》
歐元區10月綜合PMI初值由9月的53.1降至51.9，
雖連續四個月高於50、顯示經濟持續擴張，但成
長動能有所放緩。法國受到大規模罷工與抗議活
動影響，商業活動下滑，成為主要拖累因素之一，
法國10月綜合PMI初值降至48.4，明顯落後其他
歐元區國家。另一方面，能源及投入成本上升迫
使企業提高售價，通膨壓力未明顯緩解。
中東緊張局勢延宕，布蘭特原油期貨價格升至116

美元/桶，TTF天然氣期貨價格升至96歐元/兆瓦時，
天然氣價格持續突破今年高點(統計至10/26)。
目前能源價格高於歐洲央行九月的基準與不利情
境預測，歐元區通膨面臨上行風險，不過未出現
明顯的二輪通膨效應，經濟成長則面臨下行壓力，
預期歐洲央行連續升息機率低。此外，近期公債
殖利率上升亦發揮了緊縮效果，預估歐洲央行將
暫時按兵不動。待12月份利率會議，屆時歐洲央
行將取得更新的經濟預測，可據此重新評估通膨
與成長前景，若能源價格及通膨壓力持續偏高，
不排除12月將再升息一碼，將存款機制利率調升
至2.75%。
針對近期主權債券殖利率大幅走升，預估歐洲央
行將密切關注其走勢，但長債利率的上升尚未達
到表明主權債券市場面臨顯著壓力的水平，應不
足以觸發政策調整。

重點摘要
7
《融資成本上升對歐洲企業帶來壓力，然整體槓桿
率尚可控，財務體質脆弱的企業面臨較大風險》
主權債券市場的壓力令歐洲企業信用利差同步擴大，
自9月以來，歐洲投資等級債信用利差擴大31個基
點至108個基點，非投資等級債信用利差擴大幅度
較為顯著，擴大105個基點至363個基點，兩者均
回升至2025年5月以來高點(統計至10/26止)。所幸，
歐洲企業資產負債表整體仍維持穩健。儘管淨債務
/EBITDA比率自低檔回升，但整體槓桿水準與歷史
水準相比並不高，仍處健康水準。因此，債務成本
上升雖增加企業的財務負擔，尤其是財務體質較脆
弱的企業面臨較高的風險，但目前尚不至於構成系
統性威脅。另一方面，融資成本上升，企業獲利可

能面臨壓力，根據高盛證券(10/23)預估，在其他
條件不變下，債務成本每上升100個基點，道瓊歐
洲600企業每股盈餘(EPS)將減少約3%，其中財務
體質較脆弱的企業所受衝擊最大，具備強健資產負
債表與穩定利潤率的企業較能抵禦高利率影響。
《利率維持高位壓抑估值，歐股短線將延續震盪》
隨著預算協商進行、財政擔憂短線難消除，歐央12
月利率會議可能再升息一碼，公債殖利率預估將維
持高位，加上中東地緣風險未除，近期市場對美國
期中選舉後，美伊敵對行動可能重燃的預期升溫，
均將對市場情緒與歐股評價帶來壓力，預估歐股短
線將延續震盪走勢，所幸，LSEG I/B/E/S (10/22)統
計，道瓊歐洲600企業3Q26財報公布雖仍在初期階
段，但目前整體獲利優於財報季前預測，2026年企
業獲利預估相較10月初呈現上修趨勢，歐洲企業獲
利成長應有助提供下檔支撐，歐股維持中立評等。
資料來源：高盛證券，2026/10/23，中國信託商業銀行整理，2026/10。

《土耳其基金清算風波、區域雜音增多，新歐展

MSCI新興歐洲指數10月以來下跌5%，希臘、土
耳其與波蘭股市全數下跌，又以希臘與土耳其股

殖利率利差，市場擔憂法國債市風險蔓延，壓抑
銀行股回檔。土耳其股市則受到9月中旬多檔基金
爆發贖回危機所拖累，多家土耳其資產管理公司
未能滿足投資人贖回請求，土耳其資本市場委員
會下令清算七家資產管理公司管理的131檔基金，
合計資產管理規模約1兆里拉，MSCI 10/15宣布

凍結土耳其股票的部分指數調整措施，並警告若
土耳其未能改善市場誠信與透明度問題，最快將
於2027年6月考慮將其從新興市場降級。考量地
緣風險未除、土耳其基金清算問題造成的市場動
盪，維持新歐股市中立看法。
貨幣政策方面，9月波蘭CPI年增率升至4.1%，較
8月的3.4%上升且超過央行容忍區間上限，波蘭
央行10月利率會議維持政策利率於3.75%不變。
中東地緣僵局持續、能源價格維持高檔更久令通
膨前景存在上行風險，預估波蘭央行將更長時間
維持利率不變。土耳其方面，10/22土耳其央行
宣布將一週附買回利率調降100個基點，從37%
下調至36%，為時隔9個月再次降息，符合市場預
期，央行會後聲明顯示，儘管地緣政治局勢發展
令能源價格維持高檔可能對通膨前景帶來上行風
險，然而，土耳其國內需求疲軟，供給面衝擊對
國內價格的傳導效應有限，暗示有持續放鬆貨幣
政策限制性的空間。

### Page 1

赛诺菲以约 410 亿美元现⾦收购美国⽣物科技公司 Insmed ，溢价约 46% ，为三年来制药业
最⼤并购，旨在对冲核⼼药物 Dupixent ⾃ 2030 年代初起的专利悬崖。分析师认为交易 “ 争
取到时间 ” ，可将盈利增⻓延伸⾄ 2030 年代，但估值偏⾼。赛诺菲股价盘中⼀度涨 5.7% ，
Insmed 盘前涨 14% 。
摘要：
赛诺菲正以三年来制药⾏业规模最⼤的⼀笔并购，押注对冲核⼼药物专利悬崖的⻛险。
这家法国制药巨头周⼆宣布，以每股 175 美元现⾦收购美国⽣物科技公司 Insmed ，交易总价约
410 亿美元，较消息曝光前⼀个交易⽇的收盘价溢价约 46% 。赛诺菲⾸席执⾏官 Belén Garijo 称
此次收购是 " 加速增⻓的绝佳机会 " 。交易获两家公司董事会批准，预计于明年上半年完成。
这笔收购直接回应了赛诺菲最迫切的战略压⼒ —— 其头号畅销药、⽪肤与哮喘⽤药度普利尤单
抗（ Dupixent ）将于 2030 年代初起陆续失去专利保护。 Jefferies 分析师 Michael Leuchten 在研

报中写道，此次交易 " 争取到了时间 " ，有望将赛诺菲的盈利增⻓周期延伸⾄ 2030 年代，并为公
司 " 重建研发管线 " 创造空间。消息公布后，赛诺菲巴黎上市股价盘中⼀度上涨 5.7% ， Insmed 美
股盘前涨幅达 14% ，报 170 美元。
桥贷融资 350 亿美元，摩根⼤通与法巴平摊承销
据⼀位知情⼈⼠透露，此次交易由摩根⼤通和法国巴黎银⾏提供 350 亿美元过渡贷款融资，两家
银⾏共同承销，各⾃承担⼀半份额。赛诺菲⾸席财务官 Francois-Xavier Roger 表⽰，整体融资
⽅案结合了债务与⾃有现⾦，交易最早将于明年为公司经营利润做出贡献，并于 2028 年底前实
现每股收益增厚。
在财务顾问安排上，⾼盛担任 Insmed 独家财务顾问，摩根⼤通则担任赛诺菲独家财务顾问。
Garijo 最⼤⼿笔，直指呼吸与罕⻅病赛道
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。

写评论
图⽚
此次收购是 Garijo 今年出任⾸席执⾏官以来动作最⼤的⼀次战略部署。 Insmed 专注于呼吸道疾
病领域，旗下已有两款肺部疾病药物上市销售，并拥有更⼴泛的罕⻅病在研管线。
这也是赛诺菲⾃⼆⼗余年前由赛诺菲 - 圣泰拉波与安万特合并以来规模最⼤的并购交易，超过此
前收购健赞（ Genzyme ）、 Bioverativ 以及 Blueprint Medicines 等历次交易，同时是 2023 年辉瑞
收购 Seagen 以来全球制药⾏业最⼤的并购案。
Garijo 此前已明确公司的战略重⼼ —— 聚焦免疫学与炎症类疾病，以及虽患者群体较⼩但存在显
著未满⾜医疗需求、因⽽具备可观商业潜⼒的罕⻅病领域。在此次收购 Insmed 之前，她已推动
赛诺菲与再⽣元（ Regeneron Pharmaceuticals ）扩⼤合作，共同开发及商业化四款免疫系统疾

病相关新型抗体药物，同时也在主动精简研发管线，着⼒保留成功概率更⾼的在研项⽬。
战略逻辑清晰，但估值争议犹存
赛诺菲的股价⻓期以来是全球⼗⼤制药公司中估值最低的之⼀，落后于欧洲斯托克 600 医疗保健
指数， Dupixent 专利悬崖的隐忧是重要压制因素。
对于此次收购，市场解读不乏分歧。 KBC Securities 分析师 Simon Hox 认为，此举 " 战略层⾯积
极 " ，但价格 " 看起来偏⾼ " 。他在研报中写道： " 归根结底，赛诺菲买到的是额外的增⻓空间和时
间缓冲，⽽⾮ Dupixent 的完整替代⽅案。 "
Garijo 则表⽰，赛诺菲在完成此次⼤型收购后，仍有能⼒开展规模较⼩的早期阶段交易，以持续
丰富实验性药物组合。⾸席财务官 Roger 补充称，本次交易预计不会对赛诺菲 2026 年的业绩指
引产⽣任何影响。

全球⼗⼤財經事件｜ 2026/11/19 07:50 （台灣時間）

時 4 件、 48 ⾄ 72 ⼩時 0 件。
本期⼗⼤事件
1. 11/18 Fed ⼗⽉會議紀要：幾乎所有官員認為需進⼀步升息
2. 11/18 Fed 理事華勒主張 12 ⽉升息 2 碼，巴爾與戴利也⽀持續升息

7. 11/18 騰訊第三季營收年增 8% ，⾃由現⾦流負 691 億元
8. 11/18 習近平深圳迎接川普，中美俄三⽅會晤預計 11/19 舉⾏
9. 11/18 ⽇本 10 ⽉出⼝年增 20.5% ，創單⽉出⼝⾦額新⾼
10. 11/18 外資賣超台股 765.6 億元，三⽇累計賣超 1,848 億元
事件  1
11/18 Fed ⼗⽉會議紀要：幾乎所有官員認為需進⼀步升息
五⼤重點
1  Fed 於美東 11/18 公布 10 ⽉ 27 ⾄ 28 ⽇會議紀要，當時以 10 ⽐ 2 維持利率 3.75% ⾄ 4.00% ，理事庫
克與克⾥夫蘭 Fed 總裁哈瑪克投反對票，主張升息 1 碼。
2   紀要寫明「幾乎所有」與會者認為，若經濟如預期發展，進⼀步升息可能是適當的；有兩位與
會者提出，若通膨數據不利， 12 ⽉可考慮升息超過 1 碼。

### Page 2

3   「絕⼤多數」與會者認為通膨⾵險偏上⾏，原因是能源價格偏⾼與成品油供應受限；多位與會
者指出 AI 建置需求正推升科技產品價格，是重要的通膨⾵險來源。
4   幾乎所有與會者討論 AI 報酬可能不如預期，多數指出 AI 相關債務以複雜且不透明的結構快速增
加；紀要公布後 2 年期公債殖利率升⾄ 4.80% ， S&P 500 收跌 0.24% 。
5  Fed 幕僚預估明年通膨隨能源回落⽽明顯下降、 2028 年回到約 2% ，失業率到 2029 年都低於⻑
期估計值；紀要稱就業市場穩定、接近充分就業。
三⼤結論
1   紀要確認 10 ⽉暫停是時機選擇⽽⾮政策轉向，與美國 10 ⽉核⼼ CPI ⽉增 0.30% 、⾮農新增 16.5
萬⼀致， 12 ⽉升息 1 碼仍是基準；⻑端殖利率維持 5% 以上，⻑久期股票與⻑債的折現壓⼒未解
除。
2  Fed 把 AI 相關債務列為⾦融穩定議題，與⾼槓桿新雲端以外部融資擴產的⾵險相互印證；評價
仍應區分有⾃由現⾦流的上游與依賴再融資的中游，不宜把 AI 供應鏈視為同⼀部位。

3   當 12 ⽉前公布的核⼼服務價格與房租再加速、市場開始定價 2 碼時，應維持短天期國庫券為利
率⾵險核⼼並延後增加⻑債；若核⼼⽉增回落且標售需求改善，再逐步延⻑存續期間。
事件  2
11/18 Fed 理事華勒主張 12 ⽉升息 2 碼，巴爾與戴利也⽀持續升息
五⼤重點
1  Fed 理事華勒 11/18 接受 CNBC 訪問表⽰，若 Fed 不明確釋出 1 ⽉再升息的訊號， 12 ⽉就應升息 2
碼；他認為只說「保留可能」不會影響市場定價。
2   華勒說明，通膨已連續近六年⾼於 2% ⽬標且正在擴散， Fed 需要收回去年的三次降息；紐約
Fed 總裁威廉斯前⼀⽇（ 11/17 ）則表⽰情勢不需要劇烈⾏動。
3  Fed 理事巴爾同⽇演說指出，通膨過⾼、正在壓迫許多家庭，⽽經濟強勁、失業率低、消費穩
健，若經濟如預期發展，他可能⽀持進⼀步緊縮。
4   舊⾦⼭ Fed 總裁戴利同⽇表⽰仍需升息使通膨回到 2% ，但應謹慎、漸進；她指出所得分布下半
部家庭的消費仍有韌性，這既是優勢也是⾵險。

5   市場仍未接受 2 碼情境：利率預測市場對 12 ⽉⼤幅升息的機率僅約 9% ；美元指數升⾄約
103.8 ，為 2025 年 4 ⽉以來⾼點。

### Page 3

三⼤結論
1  Fed 內部分歧已從「是否升息」轉為「升息幅度與節奏」，華勒的 2 碼主張與紀要中兩位與會者
的看法⼀致， 12 ⽉會議前的通膨數據將決定是 1 碼加前瞻指引，還是⼀次 2 碼。
2   鷹派發⾔推升美元並壓抑貴⾦屬，印證短線美元流動性與短券的相對優勢；但官員對節奏仍有
歧⾒，不宜把單⼀官員主張直接視為 Fed 決議。
3   當 12 ⽉會議前的核⼼ CPI 與房租外服務續強、利率期貨定價 2 碼機率明顯升⾼時，應降低⻑久
期與⾼估值成⻑股權重、保留短券；若核⼼降溫使官員回到漸進路徑，再分批回補品質股。
事件  3
11/18 Anthropic IPO 定價每股 83 美元，⾼於原區間，估值約 1.5 兆美元
五⼤重點
1  Anthropic 於美東 11/18 確定 IPO 價格為每股 83 美元，⾼於原定 70 ⾄ 78 美元區間，完全稀釋後估
值約 1.5 兆美元， 11/19 起在那斯達克以代號 ANT 交易。
2   公司原計畫出售約 8.25 億股，因需求強勁擴⼤發⾏規模但未公布最終股數；以原股數計算募資

約 685 億美元（推算），另有科技企業認購 260 億美元無表決權股份。
3   估值⾼於 5 ⽉私募輪的 9,650 億美元，但低於數⽉前傳出的 2 兆美元⽬標；知情⼈⼠指出機構認
購意向約 4,000 億美元、散戶約 1,000 億美元。
4   定價前⼀⽇科技股連續震盪， Cboe 預計最快在掛牌後第⼆個交易⽇推出選擇權；七位共同創
辦⼈各出售近 340 萬股。
5   招股⽂件顯⽰今年上半年營收約為去年同期 13 倍、第⼆季⾸度單季營業獲利；但 Token 降價與
企業控管 AI 預算，仍是收⼊成⻑的主要變數。
三⼤結論
1   定價⾼於區間代表資本市場仍願意為具收⼊成⻑的模型企業提供⼤規模資⾦， AI 資本⽀出的融
資管道暫未收縮；但 1.5 兆美元估值已先反映⾼成⻑，上市後股價對季度收⼊更敏感。
2   模型企業的價值取決於⽤量成⻑能否⼤於 Token 降價、企業預算是否擴張，以及新商業模式能
否放量；年化收⼊與已實現收⼊、總額與淨額認列需分開檢視，不宜以 IPO 熱度推論所有 AI 軟體

受惠。

### Page 4

3   當 Anthropic 上市後⾸份季報顯⽰付費⽤量與⽑利同步改善時，應維持有現⾦流的上游與優質
軟體配置；若收⼊成⻑放緩且股價跌破發⾏價，應降低⾼估值 AI 軟體與⾼槓桿雲端曝險。
事件  4
11/18 現貨⾦價跌⾄ 2025 年 9 ⽉來低點，倫敦銅創 4 ⽉來新低
五⼤重點
1   現貨⿈⾦ 11/18 跌約 3.27% ⾄每盎司約 3,722.6 美元，為 2025 年 9 ⽉以來最低； COMEX 12 ⽉期
⾦結算 3,741.9 美元，現貨⽩銀跌 4.95% ⾄ 44.93 美元。
2   ⾦屬賣壓主因是美元與升息預期：美元指數升⾄約 103.8 ，為 2025 年 4 ⽉以來⾼點， Fed 紀要與
官員鷹派發⾔強化進⼀步升息的預期，美伊緊張也未引發避險買盤。
3   倫敦⾦屬交易所三個⽉期銅盤中跌逾 6% ⾄每噸 12,430 美元，收跌 756 美元⾄ 12,471 美元；
COMEX 期銅同步創 7 個⽉低點，為連續第五個交易⽇下跌。
4   銅價下跌來⾃投機部位撤出與程式交易，價格跌破技術⽀撐後賣壓擴⼤；資⾦成本上升壓抑⾵

險偏好， AI 資料中⼼需求題材在科技股⽌穩下也未能撐住銅價。
5   上海期貨交易所夜盤同步重挫：滬⾦收跌 2.30% 、滬銀收跌 4.23% 、國際銅收跌 4.59% ；⿈⾦
在 11/6 ⾄ 13 ⽇已下跌 4.60% ，本次為再⼀次加速下跌。
三⼤結論
1   ⿈⾦短線受美元與實質利率同步⾛升壓制，事件期未發揮避險功能，與先前下修⿈⾦短期評分
⽅向⼀致；⿈⾦仍保留分散財政與貨幣信⽤尾端的功能，但不宜作為唯⼀避險。
2   銅價跌破關鍵價位反映中國傳統需求不⾜與資⾦⾯收緊，電網與 AI 需求雖在，但無法抵銷投機
部位撤出；銅宜分批配置，避免把 AI 題材視為價格⽀撐的保證。
3   當核⼼通膨回落、美元與實質利率停⽌同步上升時，應逐步回補⿈⾦分散部位；若庫存累積⽽
電網與 AI 訂單放慢，應下修銅的配置，⽩銀維持⼩型衛星不追反彈。
事件  5
11/18 ECB 副總裁武伊契奇稱 12 ⽉升息可能性⾮常⼤
五⼤重點

### Page 5

1   歐洲央⾏副總裁武伊契奇 11/18 表⽰，依⽬前情況 12 ⽉升息的可能性⾮常⼤，薪資與物價的⼆
次傳導效應在 2027 年「肯定」會出現； ECB 存款機制利率⽬前為 2.50% 。
2   同⽇歐盟統計局公布 10 ⽉ HICP （調和消費者物價指數）終值年增 4.0% ，與初值相同，創 2023
年以來新⾼；能源價格年增約 21% ，是主要推升⼒量。
3  10 ⽉核⼼ HICP 終值年增 2.6% ，⾼於公布前預期的 2.5% （預期以初值為基礎形成）； HICP ⽉增
0.5% ，也⾼於預期 0.4% ，顯⽰能源以外的價格壓⼒略升。
4   武伊契奇同⽇發布⾦融穩定評估，指出 AI 估值建⽴在⾼獲利預期上， AI 基礎建設愈來愈依賴舉
債，市場情緒轉向時虧損可能由股市傳導⾄債市。
5   歐元兌美元 11/18 跌⾄約 1.0965 ，創 19 個⽉新低；知情⼈⼠稱 ECB 將於 11/25 討論把銀⾏最低準
備⾦率由 1% 提⾼⾄ 2% ，決定可能延⾄ 12 ⽉。
三⼤結論

1   核⼼ HICP ⾼於預期與副總裁的明確表態，使 12 ⽉升息由研究基準變得更接近政策共識；但能
源仍是主要推升因素，薪資成⻑已降⾄ 3.0% ，⼆次傳導是 2027 年的⾵險⽽⾮已發⽣的事實。
2   升息與能源成本同時壓抑企業回報，法國財政⾵險仍在，歐股維持中性偏審慎，偏好低負債、
具海外收⼊與定價能⼒者；歐元的利差⽀撐被能源進⼝與成⻑落差抵銷，維持中性偏低配置。
3   當 12 ⽉確定升息且核⼼ HICP 續升、薪資重新加速時，應降低歐洲週期股與歐元曝險；若能源
回落使總體通膨下降、 PMI 廣度改善，再考慮增加歐洲週期性部位。
事件  6
11/18 美國原油庫存意外增加 338 萬桶， WTI 結算 82.31 美元
五⼤重點
1  EIA （美國能源資訊署） 11/18 公布上週原油庫存增加 338 萬桶，市場預估僅增加約 5.8 萬桶；汽
油庫存增加 519 萬桶，餾分油增加 157 萬桶，增幅皆⾼於預期。
2  EIA 資料同時顯⽰美國週度原油產量連續第四週創新⾼，東岸煉油廠開⼯率升⾄ 2023 年 1 ⽉以

來最⾼，供給⾯改善使油價承壓。
3  WTI 12 ⽉期貨盤中跌逾 4% ⾄約 81.3 美元，結算 82.31 美元；布蘭特收在約 89.17 美元， 11/17 收
盤為 93.14 美元，單⽇跌幅約 4.3% （推算，合約⽉份不同）。

### Page 6

4   美國國防部⻑赫格塞思同⽇突訪卡拉卡斯，與委內瑞拉簽署軍事合作協議；他稱雙⽅⽯油協議
使荷莫茲海峽成為「⾮關鍵因素」，但未公布供油量等細節。
5   跌幅受荷莫茲海峽航運攻擊限制： 11/17 有船隻在海峽遭襲擊、 1 名船員失蹤；美國國家情報總
監同⽇表⽰來⾃伊朗的威脅已⼤幅升⾼。
三⼤結論
1   美國庫存與產量雙增顯⽰實體供給正在修復，油價已遠離 10 ⽉布蘭特 116 美元⾼點；能源通膨
回落有助緩和整體物價，但對核⼼服務通膨的影響需數⽉才會反映。
2   美委協議缺乏供油量細節，荷莫茲航運攻擊仍在，地緣溢價可能反覆；原油宜維持⼩型事件部
位，能源股保留低成本、低負債⽣產商，不宜以單⽇跌幅追空。
3   當美伊和談維持、美國庫存連續累積且裂解價差回落時，應降低能源類股戰術權重；若荷莫茲
航運中斷再擴⼤、庫存轉降，才重新提⾼原油避險部位。
事件  7
11/18 騰訊第三季營收年增 8% ，⾃由現⾦流負 691 億元
五⼤重點
1   騰訊 11/18 港股收盤後公布第三季營收 2,073.4 億元⼈⺠幣，年增 8% ，低於市場預估 2,105.3 億

元；營業利益 591.5 億元，年減 7% ，低於預估 665.9 億元。
2   第三季資本⽀出付款 651 億元，年增 225% ；⾃由現⾦流為負 691 億元，公司說明主因是預付算
⼒採購款，排除後⾃由現⾦流為正 169 億元。
3   排除新 AI 產品的營業利益為 833 億元、年增 8% ，利潤率維持 40% ；公司歸屬淨利 636 億元，年
增 0.8% ，⾮國際會計準則淨利 665 億元，年減 6% 。
4   雲端收⼊年增 35% ，管理層歸因於 WorkBuddy 、模型即服務與 GPU 租賃需求， WorkBuddy ⽇
活躍⽤戶逾 1,000 萬；⾏銷服務收⼊年增 19% ⾄ 430 億元。
5   傳統業務放緩：國際遊戲收⼊年減 9% ⾄ 189 億元，社交網路收⼊年減 1% ⾄ 318 億元；財報公布
後主要股東 Prosus 股價下跌 5.9% 。
三⼤結論
1   騰訊與美國雲端巨頭同樣出現「 AI 收⼊加速、⾃由現⾦流轉負」，顯⽰ AI 投資與營收認列的時

間差已擴及中國平台；評價重點應放在雲端收⼊能否覆蓋算⼒預付款與折舊。

### Page 7

2   營收低於預期且社交與國際遊戲轉弱，呼應中國 10 ⽉零售僅年增 0.4% 的內需疲弱；中國股市
宜維持選擇性衛星部位，內需平台不宜全⾯提⾼權重。
3   當騰訊後續季度的算⼒預付款轉為雲端收⼊、⾃由現⾦流回正，且中國零售與⺠間投資連續改
善時，才考慮提⾼中國網路平台權重；若資本⽀出續增⽽營收維持個位數成⻑，應維持低配。
事件  8
11/18 習近平深圳迎接川普，中美俄三⽅會晤預計 11/19 舉⾏
五⼤重點
1   中國國家主席習近平 11/18 下午在深圳寶安機場親⾃迎接出席 APEC 領導⼈⾮正式會議的美國總
統川普，以回應 9 ⽉訪美時川普夫婦的機場迎接禮遇。
2   習近平表⽰期待就中美關係、亞太合作與國際情勢深⼊交流，建⽴以尊重、公平、互惠為基礎
的建設性戰略穩定關係；川普稱此⾏將推進雙邊關係並取得圓滿成功。
3   克⾥姆林宮 11/14 宣布，普丁、習近平與川普將於 11/19 在深圳舉⾏三⽅會晤，議程尚未公布；
俄⽅稱普丁與川普 11/18 未進⾏實質會談。
4   中國國台辦 11/18 表⽰台灣問題純屬中國內政，三⽅會晤資訊將適時發布；美國眾議院另⼀委

員會同⽇致函輝達暫代執⾏⻑，要求說明晶⽚出⼝情況。
5   會晤前背景：美國商務部 11/16 已將受限制實體使⽤遠端雲端算⼒納⼊出⼝管制；三⽅會晤結
果與任何晶⽚、關稅協議截⾄發稿均尚未公布。
三⼤結論
1   ⾼規格接待顯⽰中美雙⽅有意穩定關係，但截⾄⽬前沒有公布任何具體協議，出⼝管制規則仍
有效；在正式⽂件公布前，不宜預先推算對 AI 晶⽚海外收⼊的影響。
2   ⼈⺠幣與中國股市應分開判讀：境內美元供給偏多⽀撐⼈⺠幣，但內需仍弱，⼈⾏已提⾼外匯
存款準備⾦率抑制單向升值；峰會氣氛本⾝不⾜以構成全⾯提⾼中國資產權重的理由。
3   當會晤後公布的正式⽂件涉及晶⽚出⼝許可或關稅調整時，應逐項核對適⽤範圍與⽣效⽇，再
調整半導體與中國出⼝技術股權重；若僅有原則性聲明，維持既有配置。
事件  9
11/18 ⽇本 10 ⽉出⼝年增 20.5% ，創單⽉出⼝⾦額新⾼

### Page 8

五⼤重點
1   ⽇本財務省 11/18 公布 10 ⽉出⼝年增 20.5% ，略⾼於市場預估 20% ，出⼝⾦額約 11.8 兆⽇圓，創
單⽉歷史新⾼，已連續 14 個⽉成⻑； 9 ⽉為年增 16.5% 。
2  10 ⽉進⼝年增 21.6% ，低於預估 25.8% ，⾦額約 12.2 兆⽇圓，同樣創單⽉新⾼，但進⼝數量年
減 0.2% ；貿易逆差 4,765 億⽇圓，連續第 6 個⽉逆差，⼩於預估的 9,560 億⽇圓。
3   出⼝成⻑由 AI 投資帶動的半導體與晶⽚製造設備出貨⽀撐；對美國出⼝年增 23.9% ，對中國年
增 13.3% ，對歐盟年增 22.1% 。
4   進⼝⾦額上升主要反映原油價格與運費偏⾼，中東航運受阻使⽇本 10 ⽉⾃美國進⼝⽯油⾦額⼤
增；能源成本是貿易持續逆差的主因。
5   背景：⽇本 11/16 公布第三季 GDP 意外萎縮；⽇銀總裁植⽥和男預定 11/19 上午出席國會，市場
關注其對 12 ⽉升息的說法。
三⼤結論
1   ⽇本外需在 AI 設備與半導體帶動下持續強勁，與第三季 GDP 萎縮、家庭實質消費疲弱形成

「外熱內冷」；企業盈利仍有⽀撐，⽇股宜偏重設備、材料與具現⾦流的企業。
2   能源進⼝推升逆差，弱⽇圓提⾼進⼝成本、強⽇圓則壓縮出⼝收⼊，匯率不能單向視為利多；
⽇銀下次升息研究情境仍為 12 ⽉或明年 1 ⽉，但內需疲弱限制升息速度。
3   當⽇本實質消費改善、⽇銀明確釋出 12 ⽉升息訊號時，可分批提⾼⽇股與⽇圓部位；若內需
持續萎縮使升息延後，應維持⽇股選股⽽⾮指數全⾯加碼，⽇本⻑債續維持低配。
事件  10
11/18 外資賣超台股 765.6 億元，三⽇累計賣超 1,848 億元
五⼤重點
1   台股加權指數 11/18 收 40,194.91 點，上漲 102.80 點、漲幅 0.26% ，成交⾦額約 1.03 兆元；櫃買
指數下跌 0.31% 。
2   外資及陸資當⽇賣超 765.64 億元，三⽇累計賣超 1,848.47 億元；投信買超 175.58 億元，⾃營商
賣超 41.92 億元，三⼤法⼈合計賣超 632.07 億元。
3   台積電收 2,175 元、上漲 5 元，⼤盤在外資⼤幅提款下仍維持⼩漲，主要由投信與內資承接；新

台幣兌美元盤中貶⾄約 32.98 。

### Page 9

4   前⼀⽇（ 11/17 ）台股下跌 947 點收 40,092 點；外資賣超延續 11/16 單⽇重挫 6.03% 以來的去槓
桿賣壓。
5   ⾼盛 11/17 將台股評等由「加碼」降為「中⽴」，加權指數⽬標由 57,500 點下調⾄ 50,000 點；台
灣 10 ⽉外銷訂單預定 11/20 公布，研究預估 1,125 ⾄ 1,159 億美元。
三⼤結論
1   外資連續⼤額賣超反映 AI 評價與資⾦⾯的重新定價，但台灣 10 ⽉出⼝年增 58.9% 、台積電 10 ⽉
營收年增 59.7% ，顯⽰基本⾯交付仍在；槓桿調整不等於訂單崩潰，也不保證估值已⾒底。
2   外資匯出與美國升息預期同時壓抑新台幣，股市基本⾯與匯率應分開管理；台股以現⾦流較強
的晶圓代⼯為核⼼， ODM 與⾼估值中游需檢查客戶融資、應收與⽑利。
3   當 11/20 外銷訂單符合或⾼於預估、外資賣超收斂且融資維持率回穩時，可分批增持晶圓代⼯
與先進封裝；若訂單低於預估且回款轉弱，應降低 ODM 與⾼槓桿部位，並提⾼新台幣以外的流

動性。
待確認
1. 推算值：事件 3 以原計畫 8.25 億股乘以 83 美元推算募資約 685 億美元（實際發⾏股數已擴⼤但未公布）；事件 6 布蘭
特單⽇跌幅約 4.3% 為不同合約⽉份推算。
2. 單⼀或⼆⼿來源：事件 2 利率預測市場 12 ⽉⼤幅升息機率約 9% ；事件 3 機構與散戶認購意向、招股⽂件營收倍數與
獲利數字取⾃媒體轉述，未直接核對 SEC 原⽂；事件 5 ECB 將討論提⾼最低準備⾦率為知情⼈⼠說法；事件 6 赫格塞
思⽯油協議說法未⾒官⽅⽂件；事件 9 市場預估值取⾃媒體。
3. 數據⼝徑：事件 7 資本⽀出採公司公布的「資本⽀出付款」 651 億元，快訊另有資本⽀出 814.3 億元的不同⼝徑，未
採⽤；事件 9 ⽇本 9 ⽉出⼝年增 16.5% 為最新修正值，與先前公布的 16.7% 不同。
4. 尚未公布：中美俄三⽅會晤結果（ 11/19 ）、植⽥和男國會發⾔（ 11/19 ）、 Anthropic ⾸⽇交易表現（ 11/19 ）、台灣
10 ⽉外銷訂單（ 11/20 ）、騰訊港股 11/19 開盤反應。

5. 與上⼀輪⽐對（「全球⼗⼤財經事件｜ 2026/11/18 07:50 （台灣時間）」）：輝達第三季財報、威廉斯談話、美國 10
⽉零售與⼯業⽣產、美軍空襲格什姆島與 11/17 布蘭特收盤、台股 11/17 收盤、 Anthropic 獲准掛牌與發⾏區間、華許 AI
情境、歐盟執委會秋季預測、美銀經理⼈調查、京東財報，本輪均未再列為事件，僅在相關事件中以⼀句背景帶過。
6. 新事實晚於上⼀輪資料同步時間（ 11/18 07:50 ）：事件 3 Anthropic 定價（美東 11/18 收盤後，台灣時間 11/19 清
晨）、事件 6 11/18 原油結算與 EIA 庫存（台灣時間 11/18 23:30 起）、事件 10 台股 11/18 收盤（台灣時間 11/18 13:30 ），
均以新事實為主體。
7. 72 ⼩時內無主要經濟體央⾏實際調整利率或準備⾦率；迦納央⾏ 11/18 降息 300 個基點⾄ 11% ，⾮主要經濟體未單
獨成件；澳洲央⾏ 11/17 公布的是 11 ⽉升息會議紀要，⾮新決議。

8. 48 ⾄ 72 ⼩時⼊選 0 件： 11/16 ⾄ 11/17 上午的事件多已於前兩輪列出，其餘份量低於較新事件。
9. 九⼤分類中，台灣經濟與央⾏近 72 ⼩時無重⼤官⽅數據或央⾏動作（外銷訂單 11/20 才公布）；歐洲、⽇本公司僅
Soitec 等中⼩型財報，份量不⾜；台灣公司無單獨成件的重⼤公告，以事件 10 台股與台積電股價呈現。

### Page 10

10. 六⼤實質內涵皆有涵蓋：重要經濟數據（事件 5 、 6 、 9 ）、政府與財政政策（事件 8 ）、央⾏官員發⾔（事件 1 、
2 、 5 ）、劇烈⾏情⽰警（事件 4 、 10 ）、⼤型公司財報（事件 7 ）、 AI 重要發展（事件 1 、 3 、 7 ）。英國 10 ⽉ CPI 年增
3.6% 與預期相符，份量低於⼊選事件。
資料同步時間
資料同步時間：台灣時間 2026/11/19 07:50 （快訊更新⾄ 11/19 03:31 ）；涵蓋區間：台灣時間 11/16
07:50 ⾄ 11/19 07:50 ；⽐對之上⼀輪報告主旨：「全球⼗⼤財經事件｜ 2026/11/18 07:50 （台灣時
間）」。

日本
(株)日本総合研究所 日本経済展望 2026年10月3
企業の景況感は総じて良好
◆製造業の景況感は改善
日銀短観９月調査によると、大企業・製造業の
業況判断ＤＩは前回調査比＋２％ポイント上昇。
ＡＩ・半導体関連の需要拡大を背景に、機械工
業が好調なほか、非鉄金属など関連業種の景況感
も改善。大企業・非製造業は前回調査比▲２％
ポイント低下。燃料費や人件費などのコスト増加が
重石となり、小売や宿泊・飲食サービスなどで景況
感が悪化。
◆熊本地震が生産を下押し
製造業の生産活動は持ち直し。８月の鉱工業生
産は、前月比▲1.7％と減少。熊本地震による工
場の操業停止を受けた自動車工業を中心に幅広
い業種で減産。ただし、堅調なＡＩ関連需要を背
景に、生産用機械工業が増加するなど、生産活動
は基調として持ち直し。
先行きの生産計画によると、９月の製造工業生
産予測指数（過去の予測誤差を基に経済産業省
が算出した補正値）は同＋1.6％と上昇する見込
み。好調な半導体需要を背景に、機械工業が増産

する見通し。
◆中国人の渡航自粛が訪日需要の重石に
８月の訪日外客数は前年同月比▲9.6%と減少。
中国政府による日本への渡航自粛の呼びかけの影
響が続き、中国人訪日客の減少が全体を押し下げ。
もっとも、中国以外の主要国・地域からの訪日外客
数はコロナ禍前を上回る水準に回復。

財輸出は資本財を中心に持ち直し
◆財輸出は資本財が好調
財輸出は振れを伴いながらも持ち直し。財別にみ
ると、資本財が増加し、2022年以来の高水準。世
界的なＡＩブームを背景に、とりわけ半導体等製造
装置の輸出が好調。
先行きの財輸出は、資本財を中心に持ち直しの
動きが続く見通し。海外からの機械受注額は大きく
増加しており、今後の輸出を押し上げる公算大。半
導体市況の好調などを背景に、韓国や台湾などの
半導体メーカーが工場を新設する予定。これらのグ
ローバル企業による設備投資の拡大は、わが国が強
みを持つ半導体等製造装置輸出の追い風となる見
込み。
◆企業の投資意欲には底堅さ
設備投資は総じて持ち直し。総固定資本形成を
形態別にみると、研究・開発サービスの一時的要因
などにより知的財産生産物投資が落ち込んだものの、
建設投資は横ばい圏を維持したほか、機械投資は
増加。
先行きの設備投資は持ち直す見通し。企業の設
備投資意欲は底堅さを維持。９月の日銀短観によ

れば、2026年度の設備投資計画は、ソフトウェア
投資で一部弱さが見られたものの、全体として昨年
並みの高水準。前回調査で落ち込みが懸念された
中小企業を含め、設備不足感や人手不足感を背
景に、生産能力増強や省力化に向けた投資需要
が根強い状況。

日本
(株)日本総合研究所 日本経済展望 2026年10月5
良好な所得環境が個人消費を下支え
◆実質賃金は上昇
８月の一般労働者の所定内給与は前年比＋
3.8％と高い伸びを維持。パートタイム労働者（時
給ベース）も同＋4.1％と、高い伸び。こうした賃金
の増勢を受けて、実質賃金は同＋1.5％と、８ヵ月
連続でプラス圏で推移。
先行きも、実質賃金は前年比プラスでの推移が続
く見通し。人手不足感の高まりを背景に企業は賃
上げ姿勢を強めているほか、最低賃金の引き上げも
パートタイム労働者を中心に賃金を押し上げ。今年
度の最低賃金の引き上げ率は前年比＋5.0％と、
前年度から幾分鈍化したものの、例年対比で高い
水準。
◆消費は先行き底堅く推移
８月の消費活動指数は、サービス消費が引き続き
堅調に推移。一方、食品価格の高止まりなどを背
景に非耐久財が伸び悩んでいるほか、耐久財でもエ
アコンの駆け込み需要や自動車の減税に伴う需要
増加による押し上げがはく落。
先行き、個人消費は緩やかに回復する見込み。

実質賃金の上昇に加えて、消費者マインドが足元で
改善していることも下支え要因に。ただし、資源価格
の高騰により物価上昇圧力が強まった場合、実質
賃金の下振れを通じて消費が腰折れする可能性に
注意。
代わりに最低賃金の図表を
ここにお願いします

年度末まで
継続されると想定
◆政策要因を除いた物価の伸びは高止まり
８月のコアＣＰＩ（生鮮食品を除く総合）は前
年比＋1.7％と、２％割れ。半導体市況の高騰な
どを背景にスマートフォンなどコア財の伸びが加速した
一方、政府の物価高対策がエネルギー価格を抑制。
こうした政策などの特殊要因を除いた物価の伸び
（日銀公表値）は同＋2.6％と、２％を上回って
おり、物価上昇圧力は根強い状況。
先行きの物価は伸びが加速する見通し。中東危
機の再燃を受けて原油価格が高止まりしているほか、
冬場にかけて家計向け電気・ガス代抑制策の終了

が予定されており、エネルギー価格の押し上げ要因
に。また、賃金上昇を起点とする基調的なインフレ圧
力が強まる見込み。足元では、生産性の伸びを上
回るペースで賃金が上昇しており、企業の価格転嫁
を通じて販売価格の押し上げにつながりやすい状況。
◆日銀は利上げペースを加速へ
日銀は、９月の金融政策決定会合で、政策金
利を1.25％程度に引き上げ。基調的なインフレ率
が２％に近付いているとして、利上げを継続する構
え。次回利上げは2027年１月と予想。その後は四
半期に１回程度のペースで利上げし、27年半ばに
政策金利は２％に達する見通し。
長期金利は、海外金利の影響を受けながら、日
銀の利上げ観測や財政支出の拡大観測などを背
景に、緩やかな上昇傾向をたどる見通し。エネルギー
補助金や消費減税を巡り、財政規律の低下が強く
意識されれば、長期金利が上振れするリスクも。

### Page 8

日本
(株)日本総合研究所 日本経済展望 2026年10月7
トピックス①：金利上昇が住宅市場に及ぼす影響は変容
◆変わるハウジング・チャネルの波及経路
住宅ローン金利は上昇。金融政策が住宅市場を
通じて経済に波及する経路であるハウジング・チャネ
ルは、近年、住宅価格を通じた波及が弱まる一方、
債務返済負担を通じた波及が強まっている可能性。
その要因として、以下の２点が指摘可能。
第１に、変動金利型住宅ローンの普及により、利
上げの影響は新規債務者だけでなく既存の債務者
にも波及しやすい傾向に。返済額の急増を抑える仕
組みにより影響は時間をかけて現れるものの、仮に、
政策金利が２％程度まで上昇した際、中長期的な
返済負担が相応に増え、39歳以下の世帯では年
間返済負担の増加額は平均40万円近くに。
第２に、東京などの大都市では、利上げによる住
宅価格の下押し効果が弱まっている可能性。住宅
供給の制約があるほか、人口や企業の集積を背景
に将来期待が強いうえ、高所得層による現金購入
の増加など、金利変動の影響を受けにくい需要も拡

大。この結果、金利上昇による需要抑制効果が一
部弱められ、住宅価格の調整が進みにくい状況に。
◆資産形成などを通じた格差拡大も
このようなハウジング・チャネルの変化は、若年層や
子育て世帯の負担を高める可能性。既存債務者へ
の影響は、低金利時代に多額の借り入れを行った
20～30代世帯で大。都市部で住宅価格が十分に
調整しない場合、新たに住宅取得を目指す世帯で
は高い住宅価格と金利上昇の双方が重荷となり、
住宅取得が困難に。この結果、住宅保有を通じた
資産形成の開始が遅れ、世代間や資産保有状況
による格差の拡大につながる恐れも。

販売価格判断DI 仕入価格判断DI
（「上昇」－「下落」、％ポイント）
（資料）日本銀行を基に日本総研作成 （年/期）
食料品製造業の価格判断DI
◆日照不足で相次ぐ野菜の生育不順
今夏は東日本を中心に記録的な長雨が発生。と
りわけ東京では、観測史上最長となる８月末から
10月初まで37日連続の雨天に。全国的にみても、
９月の日照時間は記録的な低水準で推移。
こうした長雨による日照不足が、野菜の生育不順
を通じて生鮮食品価格を押し上げる恐れ。農林水
産省が主産地や卸売会社などを対象に実施した聞
き取り調査によれば、東日本の主産地を中心に多く
の野菜で生育不順が指摘されている状況。一部の
野菜では既に価格高騰の兆しがみられており、レタス

や白菜などの葉物野菜の卸売価格は足元で平年
の２倍近い水準に。大根やほうれん草といった冬野
菜でも生育不順がみられていることから、冬場以降
にかけても価格が高止まりする可能性。
◆懸念される消費の腰折れ
こうした生鮮食品の価格上昇の影響は、加工食
品や外食の値上げにも波及する可能性。食品メー
カーや飲食店などでは、近年、仕入れコストの増加
分を販売価格に転嫁する動きを強めており、原材料
価格の高騰が消費者物価に影響しやすい構造に。
食料品価格の上昇が広範に及べば、消費の先行
き懸念を強める要因に。食料品など購入頻度が高
い生活必需品の価格上昇は、家計の負担感を強
めやすく、消費者マインドの下押しにつながりやすい
傾向。消費者心理の悪化が、足元で回復途上の
個人消費を一段と下押しする可能性に注意。

先行きの景気は緩やかに回復するも、下振れリスクあり
9
わが国主要経済指標の予測値（2026年10月14日時点）
（資料）内閣府、総務省、経済産業省、財務省などを基に日本総研作成
◆景気は緩やかな回復基調を維持
わが国景気は一部で足踏みがみられるものの、先
行き緩やかな回復基調に復する見通し。ＷＴＩ原
油先物価格は、中東危機の再燃を受けて当面高
止まりするものの、次第に緩やかに下落していく想定。
企業部門では、設備投資が持ち直す見通し。中
東危機の再燃を受けた原油価格の高止まりが重石
となるものの、省人化などに向けた投資への需要が
根強いほか、政府による投資支援策も追い風に。
世界的なＡＩ関連需要の高まりも、輸出を下支え
する見込み。
家計部門では、個人消費が底堅く推移する見通
し。原油高が物価上昇圧力となるものの、政府によ
る家計支援策がその影響を一定程度緩和すると想
定。高率の賃上げが続くなか、実質賃金はプラス圏
で推移し、良好な所得環境が続くと予想。2027年

度からは食料品にかかる消費税率が引き下げられる
ことも、消費の下支え要因に。
◆中東情勢の深刻化と金利上昇がリスク
2026年度の実質ＧＤＰ成長率は＋1.0％、27
年度は＋0.9％と予想。もっとも、中東情勢が想定
よりも長期化・深刻化した場合、供給制約の強まり
や輸出の下押し、物価上昇に伴う実質所得の減少
などの経路から、景気への下押し圧力が一段と強ま
るリスク。また、高市政権の財政スタンスを巡って市
場の財政懸念が強まれば、長期金利が想定以上に
上昇し、民間需要を下押しするほか、国債費の増
加などを通じて、投資支援策やガソリン補助金、消
費減税といった政策を実施・継続する余地が狭まる
可能性。

日本総合研究所主催 シンポジウムのご案内
米中対立の常態化や米国の自国第一主義の強まりを背景に、既存の国際経済秩序が揺らいでいます。こうしたなか、各国は存
在感を高めるグローバルサウス諸国との外交・経済関係の強化を急いでいます。日本も、OECD（経済協力開発機構）などと協調し
ながら、関係強化を目指していますが、現状はASEANやインドなどに偏重しており、アフリカや中南米との連携は限定的です。本シン
ポジウムでは、はじめに世界経済の構造変化や日本のグローバルサウス諸国との連携策の現状・課題を多角的に検証します。その
上で、日本がグローバルサウス諸国との関係を深化させるための具体的な戦略を議論します。

電機 
東元 (1504 TT)

調升評等(UG) 
買進(Buy) 
AIDC業務加速擴張，2027年營運成長可期 
⚫ 3Q26營收年增 16.6%，電力能源占比提升影響毛利率 
⚫ AIDC接單突破百億元，日本 MDC拓展海外商機 
⚫ 大馬達需求回升，電力設備擴產布局持續推進 
3Q26營收年增 16.6%，電力能源占比提升影響毛利率：東元 3Q26營
收 169.49億元(QoQ +2.1%、YoY +16.6%)，主要受惠機電系統亞洲及
北美市場需求成長，以及電力能源事業群資料中心與離岸風電工程營
收貢獻增加。毛利率 23.0%(QoQ -2.1ppt、YoY -1.4ppt)，主因毛利率
相對較低的電力能源營收占比由上季 26.1%提升至 30.0%；營益率
8.8%，業務擴編人力及新電力設備研發支出增加亦推升費用率，EPS 
0.75元。累計前三季營收 477.85億元(YoY +9.2%)，EPS 2.38元(YoY 
+20.2%)，獲利成長部分受惠金融資產評價利益增加。 
AIDC接單突破百億元，日本 MDC拓展海外商機：2026年資料中心
業務接單已突破百億元，預計 2027 年陸續交付，其中馬來西亞及泰
國 AIDC接單呈倍數成長。東元攜手 GMI Cloud及 KSDI於日本九州
建置 MDC，首期規模 6.4MW，預計 2027年以 9個月工期完成交付，
後續爭取 350MW擴建商機。東元負責設計、系統整合及部分設備供
應，並導入自有變壓器及安達康 Busway，有助提升專案毛利率。馬來
西亞檳城 MDC新廠預計 2027年 7~8月完工，提升 MDC產能及交付
能力。此外，東元與鴻海、NVIDIA合作推動 All-in-One MDC，整合
IT機櫃、冷卻及電力配電系統，並布局 800VDC高壓直流供電及固態
變壓器(SST)。 
電力設備接單成長，2027 年營運動能延續：東元有望取得離岸風電
3-2期訂單，預計分 18個月認列營收。機電系統方面，部分大馬達因
船期影響遞延出貨，預計 4Q26回補；受惠北美石化、油氣、礦業及
電力基礎建設需求，美國大馬達 1~10月接單年增逾 20%，北美 3Q26
大馬達與變壓器接單年增近 5成，預期 2027年營收貢獻持續增加。
變壓器方面，印尼新廠已完成規劃及發包，後續將擴充乾式、配電級
及 161kV電力級產品產能。展望 2027年，主要受惠 AIDC及相關工
程訂單交付、大馬達需求回升，預期營運成長動能延續。我們預估東
元 2026/2027年稅後 EPS分別為 3.01/3.48元，給予東元「買進」建議，
目標價 80元(2027年 EPSx23倍)。風險因子：資料中心建置放緩。
